# CHECK

Pending design decisions — deliberately kept out of the README until they are settled.

- The LSP's standard-library surface: **settled (2026-11-12) — completion and hover for every standard-library function and every builtin-type method, in every file, with the strictness surface untouched.** The gap the track closed had two halves, both structural rather than cosmetic: the standard library rides the server binary whole (`Emo_stdlib_data.files`), yet the index mounted a package only when the project's manifest pinned it, so `os.` completed nothing and `os.getenv` hovered nothing in a file no manifest claimed — the editor answered from `Ix.empty`, "from nothing", where the one surface a build never fetches is the one it always carries; and the language's own method surface (`String.length`, `Bytes.get_u32_le`, `Map.keys`, ~100 methods across sixteen types) existed nowhere but the checker's member-dispatch arms, unreachable by any index. The fix keeps the editor's answers where the compiler's are: `lsp_index.ml` mounts every embedded package into every index (`stdlib_surface`, parsed once per process, content-addressed cache never stale), with the project's and its pins' modules keeping their seats by module-path dedup — a registry republishing a stdlib short name resolves to the pinned version — and a no-project file now gets `standalone ()`, the stdlib-only index, instead of `empty`. The visibility tables (`packages`/`package_deps`) deliberately stay pin-only, so the checker's view of an unpinned require is unchanged and `missing_dep_diagnostics`'s E5006 still fires with its quick fix — the editor completes what the build will not resolve until pinned, which is rust-analyzer's bargain and the strictness rule's own: the error stays, the fix is one keystroke closer. The method surface is a hand-transcribed table in `lsp_completion.ml` (`builtin_type_members`), signatures as the dispatch arms type them, parameter names recovered from the arms' `Some "name"` patterns and the docs' own spellings (`substring(start, length)`, `compare_exchange(expected, new)`, `send_to(host, port, data)`), `T`/`K`/`V` standing for the element, key, and value; the two language protocols (`to_string`, `is`) close every entry. Routing: a bare builtin type name completes from the table the way the reserved `Syntax.` already did; a receiver no declaration answers falls to `lsp_resolve.builtin_receiver_type` (declared type of a parameter or binding, a literal initializer, a `Type.new(...)` head, or a string/char literal sniffed directly before the dot — the chain-extraction case `"abc".` names no chain, so `items_for` checks the literal route before falling to globals, the first probe run catching exactly this); hover rides `builtin_member_symbol`, an index-shaped symbol so the pipelines answer it like any declaration, plus bare builtin functions (the completion list's four entries grew to the checker's real nine — `fence` and the `expect_*` family had never been listed) and bare capability types. Hover gained the declaration's `//` doc comment (the lines directly above the span, the buffer's own text for a hovered-file symbol, the file on disk otherwise) under the signature, so `os.wait_exit_code` explains itself in the editor as it does in the source. Bare-name completion deliberately stays local: a stdlib function inserted unqualified would not compile — offering it would be the editor writing the user's next error. Verified by a three-scenario stdio probe (scratch file outside any project, unpinned project, pinned project): `os.` completes 41 signatures, `os.getenv` hovers `def getenv(name String) (R, String)`, `println`/`Bytes`/`data.get_u32_le` answer from the tables, `"abc".` completes String's methods, E5006 fires unpinned and not pinned. Six new alcotest cases ride `test/emo_lsp` (the runtest itself waits for a requested run, per the auto-test rule). Docs: `docs/lsp.md` + zh mirror (the always-in-reach surface, the method completion, the doc-comment hover).

- The `dom` package: **settled (2026-11-12) — a read-only DOM, strict HTML in, CSS selectors over it, goquery's shape in Emo's own grammar.** Two compiler fixes landed with the track, both forced by the demo's `each() -> (i Int64, one dom.Selection) { ... }` — the two-parameter trailing block goquery's own `Each` shape rides: **the wasm backend declared no locals for a closure body** (`emit_closure` hardcoded `flocals = []` while the recv-fn and init-fn emitters record `env.local_decls`), so any body needing a spill — a temp local under an evaluation-order-sensitive operand chain — referenced an undeclared index and Node refused the module (`Compiling function #431 failed: invalid local index: 2`); the fix records the same list the other two emitters do, and the one-parameter probes that had passed on wasm were exactly the ones whose bodies spilled nothing. **The beam backend refused multi-parameter closures outright** (`beam: only one-parameter closures are supported yet`, a `Lower_error` since before the width era) though a BEAM fun takes the parameters natively — the arm now lowers N parameters through `fresh_var`/`local_map` like the one-parameter one did, and `dom_demo` runs byte-identically on erl. Both fixes verified against the full suite. The track's two forks were decided up front, against goquery's own semantics and for the project's strictness rule: **parsing is strict** (`dom.parse` answers `(R.error, reason)` with the byte position, never a repaired tree — unclosed elements, misnested or stray close tags, close tags with attributes, self-closing non-void elements, duplicate attributes, a raw `<` or bare `&` in text, malformed `&#...;` references, CDATA, processing instructions, and nesting past 512 all refuse; `<li>a<li>b` refuses too — no implied end tags), and **no `html`/`head`/`body` synthesis** — the tree mirrors the source, a fragment parses into what it names, and the doc's root element (the first top-level element, skipping text and comments) is `:root`. The node model is xml's: one `Node` interface over `Element`/`TextNode`/`CommentNode`/`DoctypeNode` with a `NodeKind` enum, the element accessors raising on the wrong kind (the caller's assertion), plus a parser-assigned document-order `id()` — the identity that traversal de-duplication compares by, since tree objects have no identity semantics to lean on. Parent pointers cannot be written after `init` (E2016), and the stack parser builds bottom-up, so parents ride a `Box[Array[Node]]` cell each node carries and a second top-down walk attaches (`Box.new(parent_e)` typing comes from the init parameter; `Box` is the language's mutable cell). `Selection` carries goquery's face — find/at/eq/first/last/slice, attr/attr_or/text/html/outer_html, find/children/contents, parent/parents/closest, next/prev/siblings (+`_filtered` and `(R, ...)` on every door that compiles a selector), filter/not/has/is?, and `each`/`each_with_break` riding the language's first-class `Block` (`sel.each() -> (i Int64, one dom.Selection) { ... }`, the trailing block being the one argument) — and nothing mutates: no `Add`/`AddBack`/`End`, no DOM writes. Selectors compile once per door, match right-to-left: types (lowercased), `*`, `.class`/`#id` (case-sensitive), attribute tests (`=`, `^=`, `$=`, `*=`, `~=`, `|=`), the four combinators, comma groups, and the structural pseudo-classes plus goquery's `:contains`; pseudo-elements and unknown pseudo-classes are selector errors, `find` never silently matches nothing — strictness on the query side too. `script`/`style` are raw text and `title`/`textarea` RCDATA (entities decode, `<` is literal, the case-insensitive close tag ends both); entities decode as the six common named ones plus numeric decimal/hex, an unknown but well-formed name (`&copy;`) passes through literally — that is what the source says, not a fallback. **Language facts found on the way:** a Void def takes no `return` at all — every early-exit walker is a guard-`if` (syntax.md's mirror rule); `case` is a statement, so a def whose paths all answer through case arms is the xml-decode shape — every all-returning `case` sits as its function's final statement, mid-function ones hoisted into small defs (`chain_parent`, `anb_b`, `quoted_value`, ...); a class's method names bind in its body's namespace (`const first` inside `Selection` dies with E4004 against the `first()` method — the local is `head`); compound selectors end at any token that cannot continue them, so `ul li`'s second ident opens the next compound across the descendant combinator rather than erroring, and the selector lexer tolerates digit- and `-`-started identifiers so the raw `:nth-child(2n+1)` argument lexes through — `parse_anb` alone judges it; and the scanner split that bit twice: every text-like run needs its own stop-set (`scan_quoted_plain` stops at quote/`<`/`&`, `scan_unquoted_plain` at ws/`>`/forbidden — reusing the text scanner swallowed references whole). The `dom_demo` example walks a page through find, slicing, attributes, pseudo-classes, traversal, filtering, `each`, and the encode round trip (43 golden lines); `dom_edge` walks the strict refusals, the reference corners, raw text, attribute shapes, fragments, and the selector compiler's refusals (48 lines) — both byte-stable across runs and byte-identical on the c target. Tests: both examples ride `tc`s in `emo_project_test.ml` through the committed lockfiles, and both names joined `c_goldens`/`wasm_goldens`/`beam_goldens` in `emo_cli_test.ml` beside `xml_demo`. Docs: `docs/stdlib/dom.md` with the zh-CN mirror, index rows and sections in both `stdlib.md`s, the `docs/stdlib/` reference line in both READMEs (the zh line's drift — missing `httpd`/`valkey`/`postgres`/`dom` — backfilled in passing).

- The Emo shell (`emosh`): **settled (2026-11-12) — the general-purpose shell written in Emo, the os surface grown to carry it, `projects/emo-shell`.** The os package grew in place at 0.1.0 (the net+TLS precedent): the plumbing four — `dup2`, `execvp` (the PATH search the shell's own lookup is), `isatty`, `argv` — plus the environment half (`getenv` with unset the value `os: getenv NAME: is not set`, `setenv`, `unsetenv`) and the signal half (`signal_ignore`/`signal_default` over the `sig_hup`…`sig_term` constants, an out-of-range number refusing `os: signal_ignore 99: invalid signal` by a range guard that rides ahead of the call so both runtimes print the same text). Reasons are byte-identical across the interpreter and the c runtime by construction, not by luck: the getenv miss is hand-written on both sides (strerror has no "is not set"), the setenv/unsetenv reasons name `setenv`/`unsetenv` on both (the interpreter catching beneath its own `putenv` spelling), and argv rides a captured main — the generated c main became `int main(int argc, char **argv)` handing the pair to `emo_args_set`; `isatty`/`argv` are the plain-value kind (the `getpid` rule: what cannot fail returns plain). The new process-half names raise on wasm at the call naming the capability, the nine before them's discipline. **The shell's own load-bearing decision: every byte through raw `os.write`, never a buffered stdout.** A fork child running a builtin would lose its stdio buffer to `_exit`'s skipped flushes, and the parent's buffered words would interleave with the children's raw bytes nondeterministically — one path answers both, and it is what makes the two targets' fixture runs byte-comparable at all. **No catch exists in the language, so nothing raises:** lexer and parser answer `(R.error, reason)` and the session prints and carries on with status 2; the strictness is in the grammar — empty pipeline stages and dangling connectors refuse, `&` refuses by name (background jobs not here yet), a line may not open with `|`/`;`/`&&`/`||` — and the looseness is exactly the classics': a trailing `;`, and a redirection-only command (`> log` truncates). Expansion is the honest contract: a bare run lexes as ONE part (the byte-per-part first draft put `$` and its name in different parts and every unquoted `$var` silently stayed literal — found by the first smoke, the double-quoted form working by accident of its whole-run part), quotes resolve at lex time into literal and expandable parts, `$var`/`${var}`/`$?`/`$0` and a leading unquoted tilde expand, unset answers empty, and there is no IFS re-splitting — one expanded word is one argument, the guide states it. The interrupt discipline: sig_pipe ignored in every mode, sig_int and sig_quit ignored interactively, the fork child calling `signal_default` on all three before its exec — an ignored disposition survives the exec, a default's death is the ordinary one; Ctrl-C in cooked mode flushes the line in the terminal's own hands and the shell lives (no fresh prompt until the next line — the line-editor milestone's to fix). Pipelines fork per stage wired through pipes and `dup2`, redirections open onto the child, the last stage's status is the pipeline's with a signal death counting 128+signal; a builtin with no redirections stays in the shell process, in a pipeline it rides a fork child like everything else. Exit folds through the kernel's low byte (`exit 300` leaves 44, `exit -1` leaves 255, `exit abc` refuses with status 2). **Found and fixed on the way:** the line-start operator refusal ate `> log` (narrowed to the four connectors), and the first `pwd` golden pinned the sandbox's cwd (fixtures prove `cd`/`pwd` against constant directories only). The six fixtures pin merged stdout+stderr (one stream, `exec 2>&1`, the write order the pipe sees) and each one's exit status, running from the project's own directory, byte-identical on the ocaml and c targets in one Alcotest case (`emosh_golden`) — the `projects/` tree joining the suite's dune deps. Three example locks re-resolved against the grown package. Docs: `docs/emosh-guide.md` + zh mirror (the grammar, the builtins, the honest limits: no control flow, no globs, no background jobs, no line editor, no IFS, no `${var:-default}`, no traps, no here-docs — the os half of each already in place), the grown `docs/stdlib/os.md` + zh, `docs/stdlib.md` rows. **Found on the way, not mine to fix here: the zh TASKS milestone table lacks the M24 row** (the M24 section was backfilled in the postgres era; its table row never was — M26's row landed in both languages, M24's left for its owner).

- The `websocket` and `playwright` packages: **settled (2026-11-12) — browser automation in pure Emo: RFC 6455 as a package over `net`, then the driver's channel protocol spoken through it to the official `playwright run-server`.** The transport decision: the stdio pipe other language clients drive `run-driver` through would need a child's stdin/stdout wired to pipes (`os.dup2` exists only after M26's emosh landed it, past this branch's base) and `os.read` blocking the whole scheduler with no deadline, while `net`'s TCP gives per-operation deadlines and parked reads, and the driver's own WebSocket endpoint (`run-server`) speaks the same channel protocol the pipe does — over loopback or over a network, which a pipe cannot. `websocket` carries both halves because a stdlib that can dial but not take is half a transport: `connect` verifies the accept against `crypto.legacy.sha1` before the first frame and its header walk reads the answer to the blank line (the first implementation returned at the accept header and the leftover `\r\n` became the next frame's head — the first bug the demo's golden flushed), `accept` refuses an upgrade request that lacks the upgrade, version 13, or the key; one `Websocket` serves both roles, masking strictly by role (`masked_frame` initially omitted the 4-byte mask key from the wire — the second bug, found by the ping/pong round trip eating payload bytes), control frames handled on receive (pings answered with their own payload, unsolicited pongs skipped, a close answered before the socket closes), fragmentation joined on receive with the 4096-piece ceiling, all three length forms on both sides. `playwright` speaks the wire as the driver does: one JSON document per text frame, commands `{id, guid, method, params, metadata}`, answers settled by id, `__create__`/`__dispose__` maintaining the guid table, other events skipped (v1 has no event doors), object references as `{guid}`, binary as base64. **The facade rides the run-server's pre-launch shape: the browser arrives with the initialize answer (`preLaunchedBrowser` inside the Playwright initializer, the server in `denyLaunch` mode), the client never launches**, the browser name rides `?browser=`, and a `User-Agent` shaped `Playwright/x.y` would trip the server's version gate (HTTP 428) — the package's `emo-playwright/0.1.0` does not match that shape. Page actions route to the main frame channel the Page initializer names; `goto` answers a `PageResponse` tree (null response for a navigation that earns none, `is_null?()`), `text_content` a `Text` tree (a selector miss is the null text, not an error), `screenshot` decodes base64 into `Bytes` (on `typescript` the base64 String carrier's caveat rides it, documented); driver refusals are `(R.error, "playwright: " + text)` with the dispatcher's error wrapping unwrapped, and the engine's field reads are null-safe helpers (`field`/`guid_field`) because **json's `get` raises on an absent key — the engine never lets driver data reach a raise**. Both demos carry their own counterparty so the goldens need no browser and no Node: `websocket_demo` echoes through `websocket.accept`; `playwright_demo`'s miniature driver answers the real shapes over raw frames — the initialize object group, a skipped event, a fragmented 70013-byte document, a ping the client must answer (the driver reads the masked pong back), a 120000-char screenshot reply riding the eight-byte length form, both error shapes. Byte-identical across three interpreter runs and the `c` binaries; both demos in the lockfile suite and in `c_goldens`. **Found and fixed on the way: the c backend refused every C-keyword-named method** — the emitter's call looked the escaped name (`goto_c`) up against the IR's plain name and fell through to the refusal; `Page.goto` was the first carrier. The lookup now accepts both spellings and the emitted call names itself off the matched declaration. **Found-not-fixed, documented: the json package's `quoted_len` recurses per character**, so a 120000-char `json.encode` rides 120000 stack frames and crashes the c target's fiber (EXC_BAD_ACCESS at a zeroed page; the interpreter survives); the demo hand-assembles its one large reply around it and the json landmine waits for a loop rewrite of the escape walk. **The live check (T64.7, done 2026-11-12): playwright 1.65.0 installed globally (chromium headless shell 158.0.8127.18), and the whole ladder ran green against a real `playwright run-server` on the `c` target** through `examples/playwright_live` (in no golden list — it needs the install and a driver). Three wire facts the live run taught, each verified against the official client byte for byte through a MITM: **the driver's server answers a WebSocket accept that does not derive from the client's key** (RFC formula computed for our key, python's independent implementation agreeing with us, the server's answer matching no derivation of it — its own client never validates the accept, so `websocket` grew `connect_insecure`, the visibly-named twin of `connect`, and `playwright` dials through it; the protocol's proof here is the initialize handshake itself); **`Browser` has no `newPage` door** in this driver generation — a page rides `newContext` on the browser then `newPage` on that context; and **the per-call deadline rides `metadata.timeout`, not params** — a params budget is not read, a metadata without one never fires, so a missing selector held the driver's answer forever until the doors sent the official shape (`metadata:{timeout:30000}` on page doors, `0` on structural calls, the official client's own values). The official client's ids also turned out to be strings (`"bvhc@10"`) — the wire accepts either, ours stay integers and the server echoes them back unchanged. **Found-not-fixed on the interpreter side: the det scheduler's parked read breaks (EBADF on the next read of a healthy fd) while a call is held open across a chatty wait** — the identical ladder runs byte-green on the `c` runtime, so the live check's recorded run is the c one; the wall waits for a scheduler root-cause the way the flake family before it did. **Deliberately open, named in the docs: contexts as first-class doors, locator semantics, `evaluate`, event doors, downloads, tracing.** Tests: two `tc` cases in `emo_project_test.ml`, `websocket_demo` and `playwright_demo` in `c_goldens`. Docs: `docs/stdlib/websocket.md` + `docs/stdlib/playwright.md` EN+zh, index rows and sections in both `stdlib.md`s, README's references line, `plan/step-64-playwright.md`.

- emo-db's RocksDB half and the C ABI's byte view: **settled and implemented (2026-11-12, step 65/M28) — `Bytes` crosses a `foreign def` parameter as a borrowed `{data, len}` view on the `c` target alone, and the project-local `emo/rocksdb` binding carries emo-db's keys and values as arbitrary binary bytes.** The language decision is D10: the sqlite3/postgres crossings are NUL-terminated `char *`, and RocksDB's currency is bytes — M29's null bitmap and fixed-width row fields are full of zeros — so the `c` ABI grows `typedef struct { unsigned char *data; int64_t len; } emo_bytes_view;` (`emo_c_runtime.h`), built by `emo_bytes_view_build(emo_value)` from the Bytes cell's inline storage (`emo_payload(v) + 1`, `*emo_payload(v)`), borrowed for the call only — the C side never retains the pointer. A `Bytes` **return** stays refused: the checker reports E4200 naming the answer ("take an out-parameter (a Bytes the C side writes into) instead"), and the emitter keeps the same refusal; a read asks the length first and copies into a caller-owned `Bytes`, so ownership never crosses the ABI. The checker admits `Bytes` only in parameter position on `c` (`ffi_ok`'s target gate), refuses it as a struct-shape field, and keeps the interpreter at E3009 and every other target at its marshaling tier; four `emo_check` cases pin the clean parameter, the refused return, the non-c refusal, and the refused field. The binding is `projects/emo-db/rocksdb/` as `emo/rocksdb`, `targets = ["c"]`, `pkg_config = ["rocksdb"]` (not sqlite3's `link`: RocksDB's headers are under a prefix `cc` does not search, so one field must answer the include path and `-lrocksdb` — the postgres precedent); the public surface is Db/Cf/Batch/Iter/Snapshot over `internal/ffi.emo`, with open/close, staged column families at open (`cfs_new`/`cfs_add`, `default` required, `cf`/`cf_create`/`cf_list`), put/get/delete, WriteBatch, iterators, snapshots (the shim bundles snapshot + readoptions in one handle), and `destroy_db`. **The error split is the postgres one:** storage failures are `(R.error, "rocksdb: <op>: <RocksDB status>")`, a missing key is a value (`get` answers the sql package's 0-or-1 array), and only contract violations raise — a closed database, a handle used after its owner closed, an invalid iterator, a batch mixed with another database's column family. Writes answer the resource (`(R.ok, cf)` / batch — a `(R, Void)` tuple is not a shape, and an always-true Bool would be noise); the facade counts live batches/snapshots/iterators and refuses `Db.close` while one is open, so `rocksdb_close` never frees memory from under a live handle; a snapshot refuses close while an iterator made from it lives. Reads are two-pass (`get_len` does a real get and frees it; `get_into` repeats and copies), which keeps the shim stateless and pins no RocksDB-owned buffer between calls; the block cache makes the duplicate lookup cheap, and M34 measures before any pinned-slot alternative is introduced. **The demo is the proof, not a smoke:** `projects/emo-db/kv_demo` writes text and a four-byte value `00 01 ff 41` (a NUL-truncating crossing would read 0 or 1 byte), creates a third column family after open and writes through it, commits a three-effect batch, scans `user:` and stops at `other:1`, reads the old value at a snapshot and the new one at the latest state, iterates at the snapshot, and reopens the database to find both the bytes and the three family names; `projects/emo-db/check.sh` resolves RocksDB (default pkg-config, then the Homebrew keg), stages the registry, builds, runs three times byte-identical against `expected.txt`, and pins E3009 (exit 70) and E5007 (exit 65) live. RocksDB 11.13.2 through `pkg-config` (`-I/opt/homebrew/Cellar/rocksdb/11.13.2/include -L/opt/homebrew/Cellar/rocksdb/11.13.2/lib -lrocksdb`); the golden's twelve lines are in `plan/step-65-emo-db-rocksdb.md`. **Found on the way: a NULL RocksDB status is success, and the first shim's `emo_rdb_error(err, 1)` recorded "unknown RocksDB error" on every put** — the demo's first run caught it; the fix splits `emo_rdb_check` (void-shaped calls: NULL means 0) from `emo_rdb_error` (open/cf_create, where NULL is the failure); also recorded: `(R, Void)` is not a shape, a local `const live` collides with a `live()` method in the class scope (E4004, renamed `running`), and top-level consts used only by thunks still emit root-entry locals and earn unused-variable warnings (the demo moved `digits` into `hex` and inlined the second const). **Verification honesty:** `dune build src/emo_cli/emo.exe test/emo_check/emo_check_test.exe` green (the test binary compiled, not run — the auto-test rule); the project gate run green (`projects/emo-db/check.sh`); the byte-view cases probed through the built compiler (clean parameter, E4200 return with the out-parameter text, ocaml refusal, refused shape field); the full `dune runtest` waits for the next requested run. Docs: D10 in `docs/emo-db.md` + zh (D1's binding sentence, the architecture's storage item, the open items), the M28 rows in both `TASKS.md`s, `docs/intro/native-builds.md` + zh, the project `README.md` + zh, the sqlite3 blob caveat in `docs/stdlib/sqlite3.md` + zh and the package's own comment (the ABI now carries Bytes; `examples/sqlite3_demo/package.lock` re-resolved), `plan/README.md`'s M28 bullet and step-65 row, and `plan/step-65-emo-db-rocksdb.md`.
- The valkey package: **settled (2026-11-12) — a RESP2 client over `net`, the wire's surprises all values.** The client speaks the protocol Valkey answers without a `HELLO` upgrade: RESP2 only, and RESP3's boolean/double/verbatim/map/set/push replies wait for a door that needs them. The shape is http's: four connects (`connect`, `connect_unix`, `connect_tls`, `connect_tls_insecure`) answering a `Valkey` handle over the net surface, one connection per handle, one command one reply — no pipelining, no pooling, no reconnection. Around forty-five typed doors (string/key/hash/list/set families plus `ping`/`echo`/`auth`/`select`) each decode the shape its command's contract names, and the decode split is the load-bearing decision: **the tree's `as_*` accessors are the caller's assertions and the wrong kind raises (json's rule), but a typed door answers a server's wrong shape as `(R.error, "valkey: \`OP\` answered …")` — a misbehaving server is data, not a crash.** Server error replies ride the same tuple, `"valkey: "` in front of the server's text with the error code inside it; transport failures keep `net`'s own reasons. A missing key is the null reply read through `is_null?()`, not an error — json's null reading on the wire. The `Reply` tree merges the simple and bulk strings into one `rstring` kind (the distinction the wire draws is not one a caller needs) and carries `rnull`/`rint`/`rarray` beside it. Because the wire path must never raise, reply counts and integer replies are validated by hand — digits only, then a lexicographic bound against `9223372036854775807`/`…808` — instead of `to_int64`, whose malformed answer is the E3007 raise; a hostile server gets `(R.error, "valkey: invalid …")` for its trouble. The flat element walk of an array reply is a `while` loop (recursion there would ride the element count into the stack; a wide LRANGE is ordinary data), nesting still recurses per level like http's header walk. Request-side bulk lengths compute through `arg.to_bytes().length()` — `to_bytes` is the UTF-8 codec on every target, so a multi-byte argument's `$` length is the wire's own on all three — and the typescript String-carrier caveat for binary bulk payloads is documented honestly (the crypto-before-Bytes precedent). **Language facts found on the way:** a field is assignable only inside `init` (E2016) and there is no bare `return self`, so a chainable `set_timeout` is not writable — the door is `Void` and delegates to the conn; module-level `const`s must precede their first use (E4003 otherwise); a project entry's cross-module types qualify (`valkey.Valkey`, like `httpd.Request`) where package sources see their deps' names bare; and a top-level script statement constructing a class defined **later in the same entry module** dies with E5003 (module cycle while loading `main`) — the bare-name resolution goes through the owner index, which re-enters the module still loading — so the entry's script section sits at the file's bottom, classes and defs above it. The `valkey_demo` example carries its own miniature RESP2 server (four key spaces over `Map`, the four wire writers, a `while` connection loop) and exercises every typed door plus the null, WRONGTYPE, unknown-command, CRLF-and-UTF-8 payload paths against it — the golden is byte-stable across runs, 74 lines; the demo server's TTL answers the seconds EXPIRE was told (the language has no clock) and is documented as standing in. Tests: the example rides a `tc` in `emo_project_test.ml` through the committed lockfile. Docs: `docs/stdlib/valkey.md` with the zh-CN mirror, index rows and sections in both `stdlib.md`s.
- The `postgres` package: **settled (2026-11-11), the standard library's second native-binding package and its first database-over-the-wire client — libpq through `foreign def`, the sqlite3 model with the `net` error shape.** The binding decision: libpq carries what a wire client cannot honestly re-derive (SCRAM-SHA-256, TLS, the conninfo grammar, the protocol's own pacing), so the package wraps PostgreSQL's own C client — `targets = ["c"]`, and the manifest's `pkg_config = ["libpq"]` replaces sqlite3's `link`: pkg-config answers both the shim's include path (cflags) and the link's `-lpq`, one field, the standard resolution everywhere a `libpq.pc` exists. **The error split is a deliberate departure from sqlite3, to the stdlib's own rule:** sqlite3 raises on everything, defensible for an embedded library where a failed open is a broken program, but a network client's routine failures are values — a server that is down, a wrong password, a `23505` unique violation are ordinary `(R.error, reason)` branches to match on, the reason a `postgres: `-prefixed line carrying the server's primary message (`PG_DIAG_MESSAGE_PRIMARY`, not `PQresultErrorMessage` — the full report drags in position and context lines no reason string should carry) plus the SQLSTATE; only contract violations raise (Rows out of bounds, finished Rows, closed Conn, the wrong door — `exec` refuses rows and `query` refuses a command, each naming the other — typed reads of non-numeric text, unbound-after-close). **The FFI shape was forced by two walls, both verified in the checker:** a `foreign def` parameter crosses scalars only (no `Array[Param]`), so the shim keeps a staging between `exec_begin` and `exec_fire` — slots bound one `bind_*` at a time over a bound-mask, and a fire with any slot unnamed answers 0 (the public API binds every slot it staged, so the path is a package-bug tripwire, strictness-first); and a **tuple foreign return is unusable from a package shim** — the generated struct typedefs are counter-named per program (`emo_shape1`, order-of-registration), so `emo_defs.h` declares a symbol the shim cannot spell by name; the typed reads instead ride `_ok`/read pairs (`result_i64_ok` validates the cell's whole text in C — endptr full-consume, `ERANGE` refusal, subnormals honest — then `result_i64` parses). C status numerals never leak: `CONNECTION_OK` → 1, a result → 2 (rows) / 1 (command) / 0 (failure) in the shim. The `emo_defs.h` both-direction cc check did its designed job on the first compile: three `_ok` wrappers defined `int64_t` where `internal/ffi.emo` said `Bool`, and cc refused them before any program could run. Language facts on the way: enum members separate with commas (a newline-separated body dies with E2001 at the second member), a class's method names are in scope across its siblings' bodies (a local `var info` inside `info()` rebinding the method refused, E4004 — the local is `out` now), and the leading-dot chain refusal (E2001, the httpd entry's finding) bites demo code again — `postgres.options()` builders chain on one line. The demo rides a **temp table** (lives and dies with the session, leaves nothing behind) against a live PostgreSQL 18.6 on `localhost:5432` — three runs byte-identical against `expected.txt` — and joins `c_goldens` behind a `postgres_available` probe in the sqlite3_available/tls_cclibs mold: pkg-config resolution (default path, then the Homebrew kegs, the winning path `Unix.putenv`'d so the build's own pkg-config call resolves the same way), a tiny `-lpq` link, and one plain TCP connect to 5432 — a machine without a server skips rather than fakes. The interpreter refusal (E3009) and the ocaml-target resolution refusal (E5007) verified live; the lockfile of `examples/postgres_demo` re-resolved at every package-source edit. **Found and fixed on the way: `sqlite3` had no row in `docs/stdlib.md`'s package table** — the section existed since step 38, the row never did, in either language; both rows landed with postgres's (EN + zh-CN). Docs: `docs/stdlib/postgres.md` + zh mirror (connecting, running SQL, reading rows, the error split, limitations). **Deliberately open, named in the docs: prepared statements reused across calls, binary-format parameters, `COPY`, `LISTEN`/`NOTIFY`, cursors, per-operation timeouts.** Verification honesty: `dune runtest` was not run (auto-test rule — the demo's build-and-run against `expected.txt` is the golden's substance, run by hand); the suite lands on the next requested run, where the postgres golden should execute (not skip) on this machine. Pre-existing drift found on the way and backfilled on request (2026-11-11): `docs/zh-CN/TASKS.md` ended at M23 — the macro steps 58–61 (M24) never had their zh mirror; the full M24 section (the milestone statement plus steps 58–61, every task) is now translated in place before the M25 section, headings spelled in the zh file's dominant `步骤 NN` form (the file mixes that with `Step NN` — a false-alarm source when diffing the two files' heading lists).

- The repl's require: **found and fixed (2026-11-10), from `require "math"` in `emo repl` answering nothing.** The repl is a bare eval loop — it never runs the project pipeline, never installs the evaluator's module hooks, and `Item_require` in the evaluator is a silent no-op (its comment says scope comes from the package layer's registration, which the repl never performs) — so the require line was swallowed, `math` stayed unbound, and the first use died with E3002 (`math` is not defined): the silent fallback the strictness rule forbids, and no way to reach any package from a session at all. The fix gives the session what it lacks, and draws the line where the manifest does. `repl_loop` now walks the working directory into a project record and installs the hooks once at startup (the walk is lazy — nothing loads until referenced, and the working tree's own `.emo` files become addressable by their module path, `foo.hello()` with no require, exactly as the directory tree is the module tree); a require resolves against the standard library the compiler bundles — `repl_require` fetches from the embedded registry, materializes into the shared cache (a cache hit after the first time), and registers under the short name, so `require "math"` then `math.pow(2.0, 10.0)` answers `1024.0`. The rule the session refuses to bend: **a repl session pins nothing, so it resolves only what the compiler itself carries.** A scoped name (`acme/leaf`) or a name the standard library does not carry refuses with the new E5015 and the project path — registry packages stay a manifest's business (the pin discipline exists for published units; a session is scratch), a repeat require is idempotent, and `require "somelocal"` is an honest refusal too (require names packages; a working-tree module needs no require and is already visible). New repl tests ride the suite (the fetch path and the scoped refusal with the session surviving), the full runtest ran 397 green, and `docs/stdlib.md` with its zh-CN mirror carries the paragraph — the repl is the one require without a manifest. Verification detail worth keeping: the refusal diagnostics carry a zero span at `<repl>`, since `Item_require` is a bare string in the AST with no span of its own — the message text carries the information, and every other repl diagnostic keeps its underline against the current input line.
- The numeric conformance fossil: **found and fixed (2026-11-10), from `math.pow(2, 5)` dying on every target but one.** The checker's conformance relation carried `| Int64, Float64 -> true` — a mechanical rename of the width-less era's `| Int, Float -> true` (T09.1) that survived the width-explicit decision — so a call admitted an Int64 where a Float64 parameter stood, and the checker recorded nothing (`check_apply` verifies, it does not rewrite the argument's type); each backend was left to honor the conversion alone. The c target's call-boundary `as_native` machinery converted, silently, through C's own implicit int-to-double cast at the double parameter — the one target that printed `32.0`; the interpreter answered E3007 `Int64 has no method to_bits` from inside math's own `log` (the Int64 landed in the Float64 slot and the method dispatch read the runtime value), the ocaml target the same NoMethodError as a fatal, typescript a bigint NoMethodError, wasm a `RuntimeError: illegal cast`, and the BEAM `emo_bad_type` — five targets, five different runtime deaths for a program the checker called fine. The language's own vocabulary was already strict: mixed-width arithmetic refuses with E4004 ("the same fixed-width type"), the docs sell "strict checking with no implicit conversions", and `Int64.to_float64()` names the conversion — so the fix is the deletion. Numeric widths conform only to themselves; `math.pow(2, 5)` now refuses at the call site (`argument `base` expects Float64, got Int64`, E4004) where every other width mistake refuses, across defs (positional and named), methods, and first-class signatures alike. Fallout: none — the full runtest ran 395 green with no stdlib, example, or test program ever having passed the loose gate, which is the fossil's own proof; `math.pow(2.0, 5.0)` prints `32.0` on the interpreter, the c binary, ocaml, wasm, typescript, and the BEAM alike (riscv64 rides the kernel image path and was not booted; its codegen is untouched by a checker-side deletion), and `n.to_float64()` stays the visible path (`f(n.to_float64())` answers `3.0`). Two facts pinned on the way: Emo's `(a)` is the one-element tuple (the parser says so), so `(2.5).to_bits()` refusing with "the `(Float64)` tuple has no method" is correct behavior, not this bug; and the runtimes' own mixed-width arithmetic promotion (a local `def f(y Float64) { return y * 2.0 }` limping to `6.0` on `f(3)` in the interpreter) lives below the checker and is untouched — dynamic flows keep it, the checker is the only gate this change moves.
- The httpd Router: **settled (2026-11-09), T57.3 landed — Go 1.22's ServeMux mechanisms, Emo's shapes.** Patterns parse once at registration: `handle("GET /users/{id}", h)` carries its own method, the `get`/`post`/`put`/`delete` helpers take the bare path (naming a method there raises, naming both forms), and the segment grammar is exact — a literal, `{name}`, or a last-segment `{name...}`; braces that do not close, empty names, a rest that is not last, `//`, and `.`/`..` segments all raise, because a pattern is the programmer's contract and the wire's cleanup is the peer's business. Same-shape registrations with overlapping methods raise at the second one (the shape key marks the subtree flag and keeps literal text but only the wildcard's kind — `/a/{x}` and `/a/{y}` conflict exactly as Go's do). The dispatch cleans the request path first and answers any change with 301 (the trailing slash and the query string survive; a `..` past the root stays at the root); matching then walks the pattern's tokens with the specificity order literal > wildcard > rest deciding at the first difference and the longer pattern winning past a shared prefix — an exact pattern's trailing slash is part of the address (the bare spelling serves; the trailed one belongs to the subtree or to nothing), a subtree takes the slash or more segments (so `/static` 301s to the registered `/static/` and `/staticx` does not), a rest captures everything left including nothing, a GET pattern answers HEAD, and the captures decode per-segment under the path's `+`-is-a-plus convention — `percent_decode` (query) and `percent_decode_raw` (path) split, so an encoded slash stays a wire boundary and a literal in the value. No match answers 405 with the deduped `Allow` when some shape matches another way, and Go's own `404 page not found\n` when none does. `Request` grows `params` (init takes it; `with_params` fills it) and `serve_routes`/`serve_routes_with` put the Router in the same `Server`'s handler seat through one capturing block — the shape the T57.2 IR fix made compilable. Language facts on the way: there is no method-chaining syntax (`x.f().g()` refuses at `.` — the builders chain through local `var` rebinds inside a def, since a module-level `var` would close the typescript target off), and a package's consts/defs reach a def's body only from above — the ordering discipline that bit T57.1 applies to consts the same way. The `httpd_routes` example walks seventeen probes byte-identical on the three hosted targets; the conflict raise is a manual probe (a raise cannot sit inside a golden — it ends the process). **T57.4 closed the step (2026-11-09, M23 complete)**: `http` slimmed back to client-only (the server half deleted, not wrapped), the bench and the roundtrip examples migrated to `httpd.serve` with their outputs unchanged and their locks regenerated, `ssr_page` needing nothing (it names no server), and the docs landed for both packages in both languages — `docs/stdlib/httpd.md` (serving, the request and the response, routing, options, strictness, limitations), `http.md` slimmed, the index rows split, the networking intro's HTTP bullet split. The vscode extension's vendored `registry/` copy stays as it was: a hand-curated snapshot already drifted from the stdlib (missing `request_with` from the step-51 era), out of the build's path, not half-synced here. **The root pattern: found and fixed (2026-11-10, while verifying the web-server guide's examples).** `/` could not be registered: `"/".split("/")` answers `["", ""]`, and `parse_segments` refused the empty second segment as a `//`, so `get("/", home)` and `handle("/", …)` both raised and no site could route its home page. The intent was already in the code — the dispatch reads a bare `/` request as zero segments, and the subtree rule excludes `/` by its `path.length() > 1` guard — so `parse_path` now answers `/` as the zero-token, non-subtree shape: the exact root, nothing below it. The `httpd_routes` walk gains the probe (eighteen now), and the seven lockfiles pinning `httpd` 0.1.0 were re-resolved for the new digest. A correction to the language fact above, measured on the way: a chain on one line works (`httpd.router().get("/a", h).post("/b", h)`; `httpd_wire` already builds its options as `server_options().with_max_header_bytes(64).with_max_body_bytes(8)`); what refuses at `.` is a line that *begins* with `.` (E2001), so a multi-line chain is the local-rebind form — `docs/stdlib/httpd.md`'s options example was written the multi-line way and did not compile, and is now a def that rebinds a local. **One bad TLS client stopped the server: found and fixed (2026-11-10, the same guide verification).** `accept_loop` ended on any `(R.error, _)` from `accept()`, reading every accept error as the listener closing — but a TLS listener runs the handshake inside `accept()`, so a client that breaks it (an untrusted-certificate curl, a plain-HTTP request on the TLS port) surfaced there as a per-connection error (`the TLS handshake with 127.0.0.1:58597 failed: …`) while the listener itself stayed good. One such client ended the accept loop for good; the interpreter then answered E3012 (the main process in `wait()` and the counter both parked, no message ever coming) and the c binary the same exit 70 — measured on both, and plain-TCP servers stayed unaffected (fifty connection resets in a row, still serving). The loop now asks before it stops: `stop()` tells the counter `stop` before it closes the listener, an accept error sends `state?`, and the counter's answer — `"stopping"` or `"serving"`, `"stopping"` in the released state too — decides between the loop's end and the next accept. No reason text is matched, and the send-before-close order means the answer is already settled when the closed listener wakes the loop. The answer is a word rather than a Bool on purpose: on typescript a receive branch that returns `false` from its def reads as "no branch matched" (`ts_core.ts`'s receive uses `false` as its no-match sentinel and hands an `EReturn`'s value back through the same channel), so the message is requeued and the process parks forever — found here, recorded below, not fixed in this change. `examples/https_roundtrip` grows the probe: a plain-HTTP client knocks on the TLS port first, and the https request after it (now bounded at 5 seconds) must still answer — against the old loop it printed `request failed: timed out after 5s connecting to localhost:…`, against the new one the golden output, on the interpreter and the c binary alike. **Found on the guide's verification trail, recorded not fixed (each with its minimal repro):** (1) the **c target's `receive` cannot take a multi-return branch whose def returns through it** — a `receive` with two value-returning branches (`("found", t) -> { return (R.ok, t) }` / `("missing", _) -> { return (R.error, "none") }`) compiles to a redefinition of `__ret` in the generated C (both branches emit `emo_value __ret = …` in one scope; lab9 repro, the notes store answered `(R, String)` tuples through single-branch receives instead); (2) the **c target's `conn.set_timeout` does not fire** — a 1.0s deadline on a parked `read_line` never elapses (a half-sent head holds the connection forever; interpreter and typescript answer `timed out reading a line from …` at 1–2s; lab10 repro — `read_exactly` suspected the same, untested); (3) the **typescript `receive` reads `false` as "no branch matched"** — a branch whose def returns the Bool `false` has its value reinterpreted as no-match, the message requeued, the process parked forever (ts_core.ts's `receive` uses `!== false` as the took-test and hands `EReturn` values through the same channel; lab11 repro prints `true` then hangs where interp/c print `true`/`false`) — the reason httpd's new counter protocol answers a word (`"stopping"`/`"serving"`), not a Bool; (4) the **interpreter leaks a listening-side socket per failed TLS handshake** — after ten plain-HTTP clients on the TLS port the server process holds 11 TCP fds where the c binary holds 1 (the handshake-error `TcpConn` never reaches a `close`; the accept-loop fix returns to the accept without dropping it; serve_and_probe fd count, c clean across the same ten); (5) the **riscv64 target's codegen order is allocation-order sensitive** — the same sources built twice give different `.s` (module/segment processing order feeds some unordered collection; observed while bisecting the smoke), which makes any riscv64 golden that pins more than behavior (byte addresses, digest order) a coin flip across builds of the *same* binary and was the shape the EmoOS smoke's one-off failure wore; and (6) the **busy-port failure text differs by shape** — plain destructuring of `net.listen` dies with E3006 (`no case branch matched this Tuple value`, exit 70) while a matched `case` that raises dies with E3010 and the reason (`cannot listen on 127.0.0.1:8080: Address already in use`, exit 1) — both honest, worth knowing when a deployment script greps for one. **The guide itself:** `docs/httpd-guide.md` with the zh-CN mirror — the model paragraph (request in, response out, one process per connection), then the ladder: hello world, the request inspected, answers, routing (with the textual-not-semantic cleanup warning and the capture-to-path guard), middleware over `router_dispatch`, the process-owned counter, the notes JSON API with its four `emo test` tests, forms, static files, tuning, graceful shutdown, HTTPS, the strictness table, deployment notes — every program and transcript taken from scratch projects that ran byte-identically on the interpreter, c, and typescript (the static handler interp + c; ts refuses `file`), `emo test` green on the notes project.
- Macro tail clearing (2026-11-10): the four pilots joined the wasm/beam/riscv64 golden lists, and the verification caught the macro model's first real cross-target bug — fabricated nodes share the macro body's span, and on wasm/beam the span-keyed type tables let the second decorated class overwrite the first's entries (derive_eq's generated `self.x` read as the second class's type; the interpreter/c/ts never key anything on span, which is why they stayed green). Fix: constructor-built spans go synthetic-unique in a reserved offset range, line/col untouched. memoize_demo joins wasm/beam but waits on `Map` for riscv64 (the refusal is live — named in the list, and the trigger to revisit is Map landing on the metal). `emo dump` (T61.6) keeps the visibility promise: the expanded surface, one line per statement, with `--raw` beside it.
- Cross-module macros: **settled (2026-11-10) — the program's macro table, qualified applications, and shadowing; package-boundary macros stay open.** The macro table is one raw scan over every registered file collecting each `// #macro` def under (module path, macro name) — raw meaning no expansion, because the scan must not recurse through the parse cache it feeds; memoized on the project record. The application spelling is qualified, the same path shape a value reference takes (`// @eq_macros.derive_eq`, any path depth; the directive lexer accepts dots between name segments only), the body runs in the *applying* module's expansion, and each module's own expansion erases its own macro defs — a macro module ships nothing. Bare names resolve locally only, and a same-name local macro shadows the outside one (nearest wins, verified live: a local identity macro renaming the wrapped def answers a call the outside version would leave undefined). The macro-domain gate sees outside macros as pseudo defs keyed by the dotted spelling — real signature, a `Role_macro` directive (the window that lets the `Syntax` parameter resolve), a body returning the `Syntax` name — so arity (E4306) and type conformance (E4307) hold across modules, and E4300 names the module when the path or the macro under it is missing. The pilot `examples/derive_shared` applies one derivation from a sibling module to two classes — byte-identical across the interpreter, the c binary, and the TypeScript output. Open, recorded: macros across **package boundaries** — the table already carries every registered file including fetched packages, so the only decision left is the visibility rule (whether a package's macros need a deps entry to apply, mirroring E5008); the shadowing message for an unknown module path says "the module declares no macro" rather than "no such module" — true either way, kept for one message shape.

- Macro class application (the derive seat): **settled (2026-11-10) — `// @derive_eq` above a class reads the field list and appends generated methods; the expansion precheck narrowed to the macro domain.** The class seat: applications above `class` transform the class tree (`Syntax.fields`/`Syntax.field_name` read the fields — names only, the AST carries no field types, which is the named gap a JSON derivation waits on; `Syntax.with_methods` appends generated defs as ordinary methods checked with the module; `Syntax.name` answers a class or def; `new_param`/`named_type`/`self_expr`/`bin_eq`/`bin_and`/`member` build the generated bodies), roles stay def-only (E2031), duplicates refuse (E2032), other items stay closed. The narrowing is the load-bearing decision: step 58 prechecked the whole raw module before any macro ran, and derive breaks that — a call site naming a generated method fails against the raw class before the macro ever runs (found live: the first derive smoke died exactly there, masked until now because trace/memoize only ever wrapped names the raw module already declared). `Emo_check.check_macro_domain` now checks the `#macro` defs' shape and bodies plus every application's compile-time arguments — application carriers ride as hollow shells (bodies emptied, return dropped to Void so a hollow Int64 shell cannot trip the must-return rule, the second thing the smoke caught) — and the expanded module's full check is where a call site naming a method the derivation does not produce dies. One rule lands with the narrowing: a macro body is self-contained (its parameters, `Syntax`, the builtins; a helper is itself `#macro` or the logic moves inline). The pilot `examples/derive_eq_demo` generates an `eq?` method comparing every field pairwise on two classes — byte-identical across the interpreter, the c binary, and the TypeScript output. Still named, deliberately open: declaration-structure holes (`def ${name}(...)`) and field types on the class tree — a JSON derivation wants both; cross-module macros (`@name` through a require); the constructors cover the ergonomics today. Debug lesson re-learned on the way: an instrumentation pass that sprinkles `Printf.eprintf "DBG ..."` lines must be removed with the same care it was added — the strip script asserted on one residue and left three others (multi-line prints split across lines), and three build cycles burned on the leftovers before the tree was clean again; strip by pattern on the whole file and re-grep `DBG` to zero before rebuilding.

- Macro arguments and quotation: **settled (2026-11-09) — the application supplies the macro's leading scalar parameters, and `quote { ... }` lays the generated code out as ordinary code.** The application's argument list lexes as ordinary tokens after the directive's name (the raw-text capture retired on arrival), so the arguments carry the file's real spans and the source's own adjacency rules read them; the parentheses must touch the name (E2034) and close on the directive's line (E2033), role directives take none (E2035), and a macro's shape generalizes to leading scalar compile-time parameters (`Int64`/`String`/`Bool`/`Float64`/`Byte`/`Char`) plus the trailing `Syntax` tree parameter (E4302). Arity (E4306) and per-argument type conformance (E4307) check statically in the builtins-only environment the expansion evaluates them in — the same answer at checking and at expansion, so a name the compile-time profile cannot see never half-works. A macro refuses a shape it cannot wrap by raising, and the raise surfaces as the expansion's error E4308 with the Exception's message — refusal is part of a macro's contract, not a crash. The quote surface: `quote { ... }` parses its body as ordinary items over the quote's own token stream — real spans, no error recovery inside — with each `${expr}` hole parsed in place and a compiler-owned placeholder identifier standing in the stream; the placeholder spans the whole hole so "parentheses touch the callee" reads a spliced callee as one token (the first smoke run caught the E2003 this prevents); the evaluator substitutes each hole's tree, a non-expression tree in a hole is E4309, and the placeholder spelling is reserved (E2036). Deliberately out: declaration-structure holes (`def ${name}(${params}) ${ret}`) and holes in class bodies — the derive step generalizes the template from statements to declarations, and the constructors cover them today. Two evaluator lessons landed with it: `Syntax.seq` flattens (nested sequences concatenate — a macro composing `seq(a, seq(b, c))` is the natural shape and the splice was refusing it), and the evaluator's two recursive chains (the value walkers and the macro/builtin group) had to become one chain for the quote's substitution to see the evaluator — the plain `let` that split them was the third "an inserted definition broke a recursion chain" instance on this compiler. The pilot `examples/memoize_demo` shows the whole story in its output: a hit skips the computation, the table reaching its limit drops and restarts, the evicted arguments compute again — byte-identical across the interpreter, the c binary, and the TypeScript output. One known cosmetic warning, recorded not fixed: the c backend emits an unused retained alias for a macro-generated top-level `Map` constant (the wrapper reads it through the lazy initializer path) — fixing it means touching the c backend's constant strategy, not the macro model, and is deferred with it.

- The httpd connection half, and three compiler bugs it flushed out: **found and fixed (2026-11-09), T57.2 landed.** The server now runs one process per connection over keep-alive: heads read under the 431 limit, bodies by Content-Length or chunked under 413, `100 Continue` before a waited-on body, responses always carrying `Content-Length` (the handler's own standing) with HEAD/204/304 stripped, the negotiation closing on `Connection: close` and holding HTTP/1.0 open on `keep-alive`, `serve` answering the `Server` handle whose `stop()` closes the listener and whose `wait()` joins a drain counter process (the sync.WaitGroup shape — add at accept, done at connection end, release answers every waiter and then parks forever answering waits so no caller mails a dead pid), and `server_options()` growing `with_read_timeout`/`with_idle_timeout`/`with_max_header_bytes`/`with_max_body_bytes`/`with_date` — the clock injected because the language has none, default output byte-deterministic. The goldens flushed three real bugs, each pinned by a minimal probe first: **(1) the IR dropped arguments on calls naming a top-level const holding a callable** — `const logged = -> (next Block) { ... }; logged(app)` resolved through `resolve_callee` to the const's zero-param memoized thunk and `order_args env [] args` emptied the list, so both compiled targets passed nothing (the interpreter answered through its frame local; the c binary rendered `<block>`, ocaml `<block <block>>`); the fix lowers a zero-param symbol called WITH arguments as the dynamic `Call_value` — a real zero-param def called with arguments is the checker's refusal, never the IR's shape. **(2) The c target built `-O2` under strict aliasing and crashed deterministically** on the httpd_serve shape — `emo_release` reading a garbage pointer (`EXC_BAD_ACCESS` writing `__cstring`'s bytes, ASan BUS in `emo_cell_kind` at `0x800001fff8`), sensitive to module processing order (the same source under a different package name ran green 3/3) — while `-O0` and `-O2 -fno-strict-aliasing` agreed byte-for-byte with the interpreter on every run; the c target's cc line now carries `-fno-strict-aliasing` (riding the cflags list, so the build cache invalidates). **The underlying word-view pun: found and eliminated (2026-11-10)** — one calloc'd cell was viewed through three struct types (`emo_idhead.rc` as int64, `emo_cell.header` as uintptr_t, the payload as `uintptr_t*`), the classic cross-member aliasing the standard forbids; the cell is now ONE declared type — `{ int64_t rc; uintptr_t header; uintptr_t words[]; }` with the value pointing at the allocation base — so `emo_payload`/`emo_rc_of`/`emo_cell_kind`/the free all read through a single struct, the byte layout is unchanged ([rc][header][payload] before and after), and `-fno-strict-aliasing` stays on the cc line as insurance against whatever else -O2 may find, no longer load-bearing for this defect. **(3) Two ts gaps closed**: `listenerClose` set `closed` but left parked accepts pending — Node's loop drained and ended the program with the accept parked (the other targets fail the parked accept on close; ts now rejects the waiters exactly as its error event does) — and a receive whose branch returns a value threw `EReturn` past `E.receive` to the process wrapper, which swallows it: a receive returning a value inside a method or a callee def ended the program silently (`Server.wait()` never returned; the corpus never exercised it because sync's `wait()` returns nothing — the demo target gap). `E.receive` now answers the EReturn itself, and the emitter honors a receive in tail position (`await E.receive(...)` was dropping the def's value at the function boundary). **The second latent c-runtime flake: ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED (2026-11-10).** The hunt began with a wrong premise — the failing runs were assumed clean exits because the first diagnostic never actually printed the child's status; the measured truth was **SIGSEGV (exit 139)**, a crash, not an exit. The reproduction ladder: a C replica of the harness's fork-and-pipe execution stayed green (ruling out fork/setsid/pipes/devnull-stdin, SIGPIPE and all-signals dispositions and masks, environment padding, and inherited fd counts one hypothesis at a time), while the one untasted variable — the exit code — turned out to be the crash all along. ASan on the failing program caught it at **`emo_release` reading a value 8 bytes before a 32-byte heap block**; the unwinder dead-ended at the fiber boundary, and reading the frame chain by hand put the caller at the generated `emo__scc0` self-tail-call driver, releasing `answer_request`'s `body5` at its **first-assignment** site. The defect: **the mutual-tail-call cluster's sibling rebind jumps to the `_in` label, which sat after the member's head retains but BEFORE its hoisted local declarations — so a rebind into a sibling member skipped that member's `locals = (emo_value)0` initialization**, and the locals carried this fiber's malloc garbage (first pass) or the previous invocation's already-released values (later passes); the first-assignment `release(local)` then dereferenced garbage. Self tail calls were safe by accident (the site's own unwind zeroes the member's own tracked locals); only the sibling edge skipped them, which is why the corpus — full of self-recursion, empty of cross-function tail recursion — never tripped it until the httpd package's `connection_loop`/`answer_request` pair formed the first sibling cycle. The fix moves each member's hoisted-locals block to AFTER the `_in` label: first dispatch falls through the same way, rebinds land on the label and run the zero-init, and the retains stay before the label so the rebind site's swap-through-barrier ownership accounting is untouched. Verified: ASan clean 5/5 on the previously-crashing program, the harness case 3/3 red → 3/3 green, and the deferral unwound — `httpd_serve`, `httpd_wire`, and `https_roundtrip` rejoin `c_goldens`, the "a flaky golden is worse than an absent one" retreat reversed by the root cause. Language facts recorded on the way: a `Block` field holds through constructors and `with_*` rebuilds but calls only after binding to a local (`const f = self.fn; f(x)` — direct `self.fn(x)` is a NoMethodError); top-level `const`/`def` do not hoist past top-level statements; `while true` fails the every-path-returns check (E4017) — the parsers recurse instead.

- The httpd package (the server half of HTTP): **settled (2026-11-09), T57.1 landed — the design and the pure parser.** The server half moves out of `http` into `stdlib/registry/httpd` (targets `ocaml`/`c`/`typescript`, `deps {}` — pure Emo over `net`, zero runtime wiring, TLS via `net.listen_tls` unchanged; wasm/beam stay undeclared with `net`). The handler answers with a `Response` value, not Go's streaming writer — status, headers, and body visible in one place kills the write-after-header mistake class, every response carries `Content-Length`, and streaming is the named follow-up (SSE, large files) arriving as a writer-shaped form, not a mutation of this one. Two serve forms, not Go's everything-is-a-Handler interface — a `Block` is not a class, so a structural interface cannot cover both shapes, and `serve` (trailing block) / `serve_routes` (Router value) say which honestly. Routing ports Go 1.22's ServeMux mechanisms (`{name}`/`{path...}` wildcards, subtree prefixes, most-specific precedence, 405 with `Allow`, 301 cleanup) onto pattern strings that parse at registration — bad or conflicting patterns are the programmer's contract and raise. Strictness on the wire: non-token header names (refusing the space-before-colon smuggling shape), missing Host on HTTP/1.1, and malformed lines answer 400 and close; conflicting Content-Length beside Transfer-Encoding joins them in T57.2's body half; 431 for oversized header blocks, 413 for bodies. Percent-decoding is honest about the String boundary (typescript's strings are UTF-8 text — crypto's carrier rule): escapes decode only into printable ASCII 0x20–0x7E through one 95-character table, an escape decoding anywhere else is a 400 naming the byte, and byte-exact query values are the named follow-up behind a public Bytes bridge. No Date header unless a clock is injected (`with_date`) — the language has no clock primitive (the slog stance; `os` carries none, every `gettimeofday` in the tree is scheduler-internal), so default output stays byte-deterministic. T57.1's acceptance: `examples/httpd_parse` walks fourteen fixture heads — canonicalized lookups, first-occurrence-wins queries, `+`-as-space, and the refusal family each with its reason — byte-identical on interpreter, c, and typescript; `dune runtest` 379 green, @fmt clean. Language facts found on the way: top-level `const` and `def` do not hoist (declaration must precede use), and a required package's types reach a freestanding `def`'s parameter annotations only qualified (`httpd.Request`), while a trailing block's parameter names them bare.
- Compile-time macros: **settled (2026-11-09) — `// #macro` declares, `// @name` applies, and runtime `eval` is rejected as a language mechanism.** The two-sigil directive namespace: a `//` comment whose first token is `#` or `@` is not a comment — the namespace is reserved and unknown names are errors, so a typo (`// #macor`) can never silently downgrade into prose; the `#` role side is compiler-owned (`#macro` its only member), the `@` application side resolves the macro name in scope, and applying `@` to a non-macro is an error so the namespaces never blur. A macro is an ordinary def of exactly `(f Syntax) Syntax`; `Syntax` is reserved and compile-time-only, confined to a macro's own signature and body by a compile-time window in the checker. The body runs on the compiler's own interpreter under a profile where every effect is refused, every host-touching builtin (`rand_bytes`, the `os_*` family, through both the builtin and the runtime-impl dispatch paths) is refused, the output hook is poisoned, and a step budget bounds the run — determinism by construction with no effect system to design. Macro bodies typecheck before they ever run; applications expand nearest-first between parse and check (riding the parse cache, so every consumer sees the expanded declarations and files without directives take the fast path); the macro defs are erased after expansion; and the spliced code is checked with the module, where a wrong-arity wrapper dies with the ordinary checker errors. Runtime metaprogramming (`eval`, open classes, `method_missing`) is rejected permanently as a language mechanism — unchecked code, an evaluator in every runtime including the freestanding metal, and runtime structure defeating the type-feedback performance story; Emo's runtime extensibility units already exist (defs as `Block` values, `Map` dispatch tables, OS processes). Value-level decorators are deliberately not a separate mechanism — a macro generates the forwarding wrapper from the wrapped declaration's own parameter trees, so the forwarder cannot drift, which is the whole class of mistakes the construct exists to remove. The `@` sigil was chosen with open eyes: `@name` above a declaration is recognizable from Java/Kotlin annotations and Python decorators, and the author's stated reason stands (Python programmers should feel at home) — the single-source test passes because the form lives in the directive layer, not the grammar, and the semantics are compile-time expansion, not Python's runtime wrapping. Surface candidates rejected on the way: the `by` keyword line (the author declined it), `quote { ... }` quasiquote (deferred to the step-2 ergonomics work with the `Syntax` constructor API landing first), and application arguments `@memoize(100)` (the lexer captures the raw text; the parser refuses until compile-time argument checking lands). Pilot: `examples/trace_demo` — a `trace` macro wrapping two defs, byte-identical output across the golden run (379 tests green). Later steps: quotation surface + arguments + `@memoize` (step 2), `@derive` on classes + cross-module macros (step 3) — tracked in `design/macros.md`.

- File IO on the wasm target: **settled (2026-11-08) — syscalls + fetch, the host formats the reasons whole, and the os package ships in halves.** Amends step 16's T16.5 trail on the file half (sockets stay refused). The protocol: a syscall import takes its string arguments as `(ptr, len)` pairs the guest wrote through `write_bytes` and answers `(tag, …)` — tag 0 ok, tag 1 error — stashing the payload (the contents, or the whole reason text the host formatted against a Node error-code → strerror table byte-identical with the c runtime's `emo_os_fail`/`emo_file_*`); the guest then calls `fetch(buf, cap)` to copy the stash into a buffer a new `alloc` runtime bump-allocated. Two calls, because `os.read` is not idempotent — a fixed scratch the host writes into (the `float_str` convention) is safe to re-run, a read is not — and it retires the fixed scratch's size ceiling. Count-bearing syscalls answer `(tag, i64)` with the payload size riding the count on the error path. The os package's manifest gains `wasm` for the whole package; the fd, directory, and working-directory half lowers to the syscalls; the process half (`getpid`, `getppid`, `fork`, `waitpid`, `pipe`, `execv`, `_exit`) lowers to a run-time raise naming the capability — the interpreter's run-time refusals are the precedent, a compile-time refusal would have killed the whole package (its own bodies call the process names), and the package-level resolution refusal is coarser than the capability. The canonical host (`src/emo_codegen/wasm/wasm_host.mjs`) rides the compiler as generated data: `emo run --target wasm` writes it beside a temp copy of the artifact and runs it under Node (the qemu-style refusal when Node is missing), and the golden tests run the same source — the build artifact contract unchanged, `.wasm` + `.wat` and nothing else (step 25's rule). The bump base moves to 65536 so the exchange cursor never reaches `float_str`'s scratch at 60000.
- Mixin mechanism: **settled (2026-10-06) — not added.** An `include`-style mixin is inheritance renamed: a single include is single inheritance, multiple includes are multiple inheritance, and both drag in method-resolution order, diamond ambiguity, implicit method provenance, `self` / field coupling, and the fragile-base problem — exactly what the no-inheritance decision rejected. Interfaces with default method bodies are the same trap and are also excluded. Reuse stays with duck-typed functions, composition, and structural interfaces. Delegation is not a separate mechanism either: forwarding to a contained object is ordinary composition code — `def draw() { return self.inner.draw() }` — and gets no construct, keyword, or auto-forwarding, because it expresses nothing the ordinary methods cannot and would only hide method provenance. Struct embedding is the same space under another name: Go's anonymous embedding promotes an inner type's methods and fields into the outer one, which is inheritance in effect — promotion, shadowing, and a depth-based resolution rule together are a hidden method-resolution order — and it leaves the outer type's method set invisible at its declaration. It also amplifies *accidental interface satisfaction*: because Emo's interfaces are structural (satisfaction by shape, no `implements`), a promoted method can make a type satisfy an interface its author never intended, and a later change to the embedded type silently flips that satisfaction. Go's embedding of `sync.Mutex` — which exposes `Lock`/`Unlock` and satisfies `sync.Locker` without the author asking — is the canonical example. Emo's safeguard is that a class's method set is exactly its own `def`s, so satisfaction changes only when its own body changes; the residual coincidental-name hazard is inherent to structural typing and is mitigated by small, clearly named interfaces. The whole inheritance-shaped space — mixin, include, default methods, delegation, embedding — is therefore closed: reuse is duck-typed functions, composition, and structural interfaces, plus plain code.
- Error handling: **settled (2026-10-11) — no catch construct; expected failures are values, violations raise.** Supersedes the 2026-10-06 `begin`/`catch`/`ensure` settle. The deciding evidence is the backends: `interp`/`ocaml`/`ts`/`beam` get host exceptions for free, but `c` needs `setjmp`/`longjmp` plus a drain of the refcount/autorelease state along every unwound frame, wasm's raise is a host `abort` no in-wasm handler can resume from, and `riscv64` refuses `raise` outright — six targets, six stack disciplines, a permanent two-tier language for one construct. The Erlang split takes its place, and Emo already runs its other half (an uncaught exception kills only the offending process, the per-process arena reclaims its memory, supervision is library-level): an **expected failure** — parse errors, IO, network, not-found, anything the data can trigger — is an ordinary value, the call returning `(R.ok, value)` or `(R.error, reason)`, the reason a String carrying the message convention (package prefix: `json: expected array element at byte 3`); a **violation** — a broken contract, a bug — raises and ends the process. `Exception` stays the language's one exception class — no subclasses (Emo has no inheritance), the name reserved against user shadowing — carrying `message` and an optional `data` Map whose role is machine-readable context for crash reports and supervision, never type identity: nothing dispatches on exceptions when nothing catches them, so the `e.is(Timeout)` narrowing idea dies with the catch design. The `raise` operand must be an `Exception` instance (anything else gets its own E-code), and the uncaught report prints non-empty `data` alongside the message. `R { ok, error }` is a built-in enum (root-env binding, the `Exception` precedent) so matching a returned tuple never costs an import; the tag is spelled `R.ok`/`R.error` — a bare lowercase `ok` in a pattern is a binding and would match everything, and atoms (`:ok`) were rejected as a new value type across all targets for what the enum already expresses. The name's one shadow — the R language of statistical computing — is cosmetic: the two never share an artifact (that R lives as the `/usr/bin/R` command, `.R` files, and CRAN; Emo's `R` is a type name always spelled in qualified form — `R.ok`, `(R, String)` — and no Emo command, file extension, or package carries the bare letter), a single-letter identifier inside another language raises no trademark question, and the fingerprint test passes — enum-dot tags over tuples are the Erlang shape, and R has no such syntax (its counterpart concept is the factor). Migration is the follow-up: the eight raising packages (~220 raise sites) and the runtime-backed packages (`os`/`file`/`net`) split per call into tuple returns and keep-raising violations.
- Binding patterns: **settled (2026-10-11)** — `const` and `var` take a full pattern, not just a name: `const (R.ok, body) = file.read(path)`. A pattern that does not match is a runtime error — the same rule as a `case` scrutinee matching no branch — making the assert-match binding (Erlang's `{ok, B} = f(X)`) the expect-success fast path of the tuple convention. No `match` keyword: the construct is a binding, and `case` already owns the pattern vocabulary. Lowered at IR level by desugaring to a one-arm `case` wrapping the rest of the block, so the six backends see only existing nodes; the parser's `const (` form is a hard error today, so the extension breaks no grammar.
- Type layering: **settled (2026-10-11) — three tiers, two promotion criteria, the freestanding target as the accretion stop.** **"Built-in" names what belongs to the language itself** — the spec core's value model, the capability types, `R`, and `Exception` — the mechanisms and types a program uses without importing anything. Two axes are independent of that ownership and must not be read as defining it: **name availability**, because the checker's root environment also carries the runtime implementations the standard library's Emo source calls (`os_read`, `file_write`, `net_connect`) — those are the runtime's implementation of a package, reachable without `require` but never the API, and no document presents them as a surface; and **per-target capability** (the target-capability table — the split already exists: `riscv64` refuses `raise` and refuses `Map` at emit time while the names stay import-free). **Spec core**: the value model the language's own semantics depend on — `Bool`/`Char`/`Int64`/`Byte`/`Float64`/`String`/`Array`/`Tuple`/enum/class/interface, plus `R` (it is an enum); a target that cannot implement the tier is not an Emo target. **Capability builtins**: `Map`/`List`/`Box`/`Bytes`/`Pid`/the connection types/`Exception` — import-free names, gated per target, the tier **frozen** (its status is the checker's inherited type vocabulary, not a precedent; refusing where unimplementable is the tier's normal shape). **Stdlib**: every future type lands there by default. Promotion needs (a) *syntax dependence* — a syntactic construct's own operand type (`Exception` qualifies through `raise`; nothing else does), or (b) *universal dependence plus trivial implementability on every target including freestanding* (`R` qualifies: every failing call returns it, and it lowers to a tagged word). The freestanding target is the standing reason the capability tier stays frozen: each type promoted above the spec core must either be re-implemented in assembly for `riscv64` or publicly refused, fragmenting the language.
