- The dual-run parity harness (EmoOS T3.8): **implemented (2026-10-27) — the console service runs on the hosted interpreter against a fake device and on the metal under QEMU against the real UART, the two legs' outputs are asserted byte-identical, and the kernel's `run.sh` smoke runs inside `dune test`.** The decisions, in order. **The corpus is two example packages, `parity_fake` and `parity_metal`, sharing one service block held byte-identical by cut markers** — the block (a `Dev` interface over ready/read/send, the line-assembly and echo service) is the single source of truth for the logic, and the harness asserts the prefix between the markers byte-for-byte across both files; there are no local path dependencies to require a file across packages, and the guard makes the duplication loud instead of silent. **The device face is what differs**: the fake is an array of preloaded bytes in, a builder out, cursors in boxes; the metal is the 16550A's line-status register gating the read, the receive buffer answering the byte, and the echo appending into a raw output ring above the heap — the SPSC shape the kernel's console uses. **The metal's hot receive loop lives in fully typed top-level defs** — class-method bodies keep the dynamic convention, and a boxed poll in a spin would burn the heap (the T1.8 lesson, re-learned at the interface boundary); the native body polls in five instructions and allocates nothing. **The output is deliberately numeric** — the per-drain totals and the echoed byte codes — because the harness's contract is byte-identity, and numbers printed through the interpreter's output capture and through the SBI console compare exactly. **The harness** (`test/emo_os`, an Alcotest suite wired into `dune runtest`): the guard test above; the metal leg builds `parity_metal`, boots under QEMU with the serial input on a named pipe written only after the greeting is on the wire (OpenSBI takes the UART first — the T3.5 lesson), feeds the same twelve bytes the fake preloads, renders the payload after OpenSBI's banner with CRLF folded back to LF, and asserts byte-identity with the same expected file the hosted leg answers; and the **kernel smoke**: `projects/emo-os/run.sh` runs inside the suite — the kernel builds into a scratch copy of the project tree, and run.sh's own assertions (the tick proof on both timer transports, the five console deliveries, the echo, the self-exit) decide the test, so CI boots the kernel on every `dune test`. **Found and fixed on the way — the interface dispatch table on `riscv64` carried one entry per class: the interface's first method's address only.** The dispatch indexed the table by the class id alone, so every method call through an interface jumped to the first method's address — single-method interfaces (every golden that used one, objects and language_tour included) worked by accident, and a two-method interface sent `send` to `read`, which spun on the emptied FIFO while the caller's per-iteration boxing burned the heap: the burn this task's corpus caught. The table now carries one slot per interface method in the interface's own order, the dispatch multiplies the class id by the row width and reads the method's slot, and a single-method interface emits the same jump shape it always did — all twelve riscv64 QEMU goldens byte-identical through the fix. **Found, not fixed: `do builtin()` lowers as a spawn** (T3.7's note), and **the riscv64 target refuses array-element assignment (E2015)** — the corpus's shared service is written without either.

- The user ELF profile (EmoOS T4.1): **implemented (2026-10-27) — the second riscv64 image shape: a U-mode ELF the kernel embeds, loads, and enters through `sret`.** The decisions, in order. **The shape is the compiler's, and a profile flag names it**: `emo build --profile user` beside the default `kernel` — the user profile selects the compiler's U-mode boot stub (no hart check: a0 is the kernel's to leave garbage in; .bss clear, own stack, `emo_runtime_init`, `emo_program`, then the shutdown ecall, which lands in the kernel), its own load address, and its own sizing; any other name refuses by name, the user profile off any other target refuses (an image shape its target cannot emit is an error, not a silently ignored flag), and a package's own `runtime.S`/`link.ld` still ride in above either profile (T3.1 unchanged — the tree owns the image when it says so). **The user image is a guest, and the sizing says so**: one 64 KiB stack — the per-process stack size — over an 8 MiB heap, the process world unchanged, linking at 0x87100000, the pre-SV39 user window's base — a page above the kernel's fixed rings, under the top of the board's 128 MiB, the whole image's .bss (~12.4 MiB) inside the window; per-process quotas arrive with the arenas (T5), and SV39 replaces the fixed window with address spaces (M5). The kernel profile emits byte-for-byte what it did. **The kernel loads the user — in Emo**: the image rides in the kernel (`user.S` embeds the built ELF with `.incbin`, the assembler's include search resolving the path, so the build runs from anywhere), the boot stub parks its bounds in a new handoff page (`.emo_user` at 0x87002000, the link.ld's third fixed section) and zeroes the entry word, and the loader (`user.emo`) is ordinary typed Emo — the ELF64 header parsed through `peek`/`peek8` (magic, class, machine, a sane program-header table), each `PT_LOAD` copied into the window byte by byte with its tail zeroed (the user image's .bss — stack and heap included), every segment bounded to the window, the entry parked; fully typed defs keep the copy loop at one volatile load and store per step, zero allocation, and every refusal is loud with nothing staged (the entry word stays zero and nothing enters). **Entry is the resume stub's sibling**: `emo_os_user_boot` reads the parked entry — zero returns to the stub's poweroff — and srets with `sstatus` keeping what the kernel left except the three bits entry owns: SPP cleared (sret lands in U-mode), SPIE set (S-mode interrupts return on), FS set Dirty (the floats run). **The gate's door exists before the gate**: the shim answers exactly one U-mode ecall — a7 8, the shutdown request the user stub exits with — by taking the SBI poweroff itself, and any other verb is the loud failure (`a user ecall the gate does not answer`); T4.2 replaces the single answer with the dispatch. Ticks land during U-mode and round-trip through the same shim — the frame on the user's own stack, the root slot's quota 0, dormant — and the illegal-instruction probe's state settles to 3 after init, so a user program's stray illegal instruction is the loud failure it looks like, never the probe's answer (T3.6's probe window was kernel-only; user code makes the ambiguity real). **Found and fixed on the way: `collect_files` walked through nested packages** — the module walk has always left a directory holding its own `package.emo` out entirely, the shape walk did not, and the kernel tree's user programs would have leaked their `.S` into the kernel's image (two candidates for one component, E5013); the walk now applies the same rule, pinned in the project suite. The pins: `run.sh` grew the serial chain — the five console deliveries, then `user: staged` with the window's base as the entry, then the user program's own greeting `U-MODE 500500` (explicit bytes plus a computed sum: U-mode has no console service yet, and riscv64 refuses array-element assignment, so the digits ride a recursion) — under both timer transports; the profile suite pins the two refusals, the two load addresses, and the user stub and sizing in the emitted text; the emo_os harness builds hello (profile user) into the scratch tree before the kernel, exactly as the README recipe does. All twelve riscv64 QEMU goldens byte-identical; `emo_cli` 325 green.

- The ecall gate (EmoOS T4.2): **implemented (2026-10-27) — the ABI is fixed and documented (`projects/emo-os/docs/syscalls.md`, with the zh-CN mirror), the kernel dispatches through ordinary typed Emo, and every pointer argument passes the supervisor's range-and-permission checks before the gate acts.** The decisions, in order. **The user side exists before the gate can**: `ecall(verb, r0..r5)` joins the raw-machine family — checker-registered on `riscv64` alone, refused by name everywhere else (E4028, the peek/poke admission rule; the refusal names the machine's gate rather than raw memory) — lowering in a native body to the inline instruction with **no boxing** (the seven words evaluate, spill, and load raw: the first cut reused `emit_args_from`, whose contract is feeding dynamic callees, and boxed every argument — the very first boot caught the gate receiving heap-cell pointers as its verb, refusing everything, and the user program exiting through a `wfi` it had never reached), and in a dynamic body to a runtime helper (`emo_sys_ecall`) that decodes the seven cells, runs the instruction, and boxes the answer. **The answer convention is one register, chosen for totality**: `a0 >= 0` is the payload (`write` answers the byte count), `a0 < 0` is a refusal whose reason is `-a0` — 1 unknown verb, 2 out of range, 3 no permission, 4 bad argument. A refusal is an answer, not a fault: the caller's registers round-trip untouched and execution resumes after the `ecall` — the gate refuses, the program decides; "kills only itself" is the process service's (T4.4) and the goldens' (T4.6). **The verb set starts at two, and exit is verb 0 because it never returns**: a handler that cannot return has no return path to marshal, so the trap entry takes verb 0 directly (the SBI poweroff, the T4.1 shutdown verb 8 retired) before the dispatcher; verb 1 `write(buf, len)` rides the kernel's own polled console byte by byte — syscall dispatch is core kernel code called directly, the architecture's own rule, and the console service process arrives with T4.4. **The dispatch is the shim's two instructions plus Emo**: `gate.dispatch` (module `gate`, fully typed — it runs inside the trap on the caller's own stack, SIE masked as any trap handler, and allocates nothing) receives the verb and six words loaded from the trap frame, and the shim stores its one answer into the frame's `a0` slot, advances the frame's `sepc` past the `ecall` — **an `ecall` is a synchronous exception, and without the +4 the same instruction re-executes forever** (the second thing the first boot caught: three ecalls at one epc, the program never advancing) — and srets home. Ticks during the gate nest through the same shim and round-trip. **The checks are the loader's record, not ad-hoc address lists**: staging now parks a segment table in the handoff page (eight slots of start, end, ELF `p_flags` — one per loaded `PT_LOAD`), and every pointer argument must lie inside **one** segment carrying the permission the access needs — the range check written overflow-safe (`len > end - buf`, never `buf + len > end`), a call spanning segments refused rather than split, a zero length in a writable segment answered, and the reason honest: covered-but-not-writable is 3, covered-by-nothing is 2. The pinned demo is the refusal speaking through the gate that refused it: hello's serial line grew `gate 500500 r3 r2` — one `write` that answered (the line's own bytes, from a buffer the program poked into its own data segment — a user program has no address-of, and Bytes is refused on riscv64, so raw poking is the honest buffer) and two refusals, the text segment (reason 3, the loader's `p_flags` doing the work) and the kernel's memory (reason 2), under both timer transports with the stub exiting through verb 0. The `ecall` refusals are pinned at check time beside the rest of the raw family; all twelve riscv64 QEMU goldens byte-identical; full `dune runtest` green.

- The syscall stub package (EmoOS T4.3): **implemented (2026-10-27) — `syscall`, in the bundled registry, admitting `riscv64` alone: the typed Emo surface over the raw ecall, and the only thing user code sees of the boundary.** The decisions, in order. **The package is a bundled-registry package, and the layering gate learned the profile**: T3.2's E5014 refused every bundled require on riscv64 because the target was the kernel's alone; the user profile makes riscv64 an ordinary program target, so `compile_inputs` now carries the profile — the kernel profile keeps the strict rule untouched (a bundled require refuses E5014, **`syscall` included**: the kernel builds on `core` alone and never requires its own userland — the gate covers the gate's own stub), while the user profile stands it down and the packages' own target declarations govern: a hosted-only require (`json` on riscv64) gets past the layering gate and refuses at the solve, by the solve's own answer, never E5014's. **The surface is two stubs, and the error slot is the convention's**: `syscall.exit()` (the exit verb, never returns) and `syscall.write(buf, len)` answering `(R.ok, the byte count)` or `(R.error, why)` — the gate's numeric reasons translated into the messages the error convention demands, because `(R.error, why)` binds `why` as the String it is whatever the ok slot carries (the checker's reading rule; a stub returning numeric reasons would type-confuse every consumer the convention types as String). The raw `ecall` lives in those two bodies and nowhere else a user program writes — no raw passthrough def, the stub module is the boundary. **The demo reaches the gate only through the stubs**: hello pins `syscall`, and the pinned `gate 500500 r3 r2` line rides `syscall.write` — the refusal messages mapped back to the ABI's numbers for the line, the mapping living in hello with the doc's name on it. **Found on the way, working as designed**: the lockfile's checksum caught the stub changing under a pinned version — `checksum mismatch for syscall@0.1.0: the lockfile records … but the bundled standard library serves content digesting to …` — and the refresh is the explicit regeneration path (a fresh resolve at first build writes the new lock). **Noted, not fixed: `emo deps resolve` resolves under the `c` target alone**, so a riscv64-only project's lockfile cannot be regenerated by that command — the first build resolves correctly, which is the path this tree uses. The pins: two new layering tests (the user profile standing the gate down; the kernel profile refusing `syscall`), the emo_os smoke building hello through the dependency with the lockfile in the scratch copy, and the QEMU boots byte-identical through the stub switch; all twelve riscv64 QEMU goldens byte-identical; full `dune runtest` green.

- The kernel services as processes (EmoOS T4.4): **implemented (2026-10-27), the exit's slot reclamation closing the task (2026-10-28) — two ordinary Emo service processes behind the gate, the console service (write/read) and the process service (spawn/exit/wait), reached by routing, answering targeted.** The decisions, in order. **The gate is also the router**: the dispatch answers what needs no service and returns a route, `-(1000 + op)`, for the rest; the shim packs the request word — `op << 32 | caller-pid`, the reply's addressee riding the request so the answer lands on the caller that asked, not on whichever state-5 slot a scan finds first — posts it to the service (`emo_gate_post`: enqueue, wake, no switch), and parks the caller state 5, its trap frame the context; the service's `gate_reply(target, answer)` lands the word in that exact parked frame's a0 slot, flips 5 → 1, and pushes. An ordinary send never wakes a 5, and a reply to a pid not parked in one is the loud service bug. **The services are ordinary Emo**: `console_service` — the write verb prints through the polled driver and answers the count, the read verb answers the next console byte through the device-wait, the boot demo's bare-pid readers ride the same loop with the kinds separated by the request word's height — and `process_service` — spawn carves a 64 KiB stack above the user image and starts the child at the image's program, refusing −5 when the stacks run out; exit records `(pid, code)` in the exit table and answers a waiting parent; wait answers from the table or parks holding the pending-wait pair, one wait transaction at a time (the stated bound). Each announces its pid to the root, which parks the raw numbers in the handoff cells the shim posts by. **Three raw builtins join the family** (riscv64 alone, E4028 elsewhere): `user_spawn(entry, stack)` — the child's context is a synthetic trap frame on its own stack, every register zero, SPIE with SPP clear and FS dirty, sepc the entry, the ra slot the user-exit stub so a top-level fall-off exits; the stub lives in the compiler's runtime body because the user profile assembles without the kernel package, and the slot's sp word stores the frame's switch-section base (F + 336) per the switch-in protocol; `gate_reply(target, answer)` — two-argument, targeted; and `pid_num(Pid)`. **Three faults the spawn/wait round trip caught and fixed** (the failure record lived in `docs/temp-spawn-wait-problem.md`, retired at landing): ① the child's first dispatch `ret`ted to 0 — its slot held the trap frame's base where the switch-in protocol reads the switch-section base, so the switch-in restored 208 bytes of zeros and returned through the frame's zeroed ra (cause 1, epc 0x0, the exact signature); ② with two transactions parked at once (the parent's wait beside the child's write), the reply-to-first-state-5-slot scan and the shared argument cells broke deterministically — the reply rebuilt targeted, the argument cells split per service, the process service holds `pending_waiter` beside `pending_target`; ③ the shim's answer test `a0 >= −1000` swallowed `route_process_exit`, which *is* −1000 (op 0) — the child's exit "returned" −1000 and the child fell through into the parent's proof code; strict `>` now, the exit a route like its siblings. **The close-out — the exit's slot reclamation is the death made honest**: the exit is the one route that never answers, so the shim's exit route marks the caller's slot done (state 3, the same state a returning entry thunk writes) instead of parking it as a gate-wait no reply would ever relieve — the slot stops claiming a transaction at the ecall itself, a send to the exited pid drops instead of queueing into a mailbox nobody drains, a stray reply is the loud service bug instead of a resurrection (the zombie at 5 was resurrectable: a reply flipped it alive past its own exit), and the frame stays on the dead process's own stack for T5's arena reclamation to take back. **The exit table holds eight unreaped exits**; a ninth is dropped, and its eventual waiter walks into the loud blocked deadlock — the stated bound, the full-table rework its own piece if it ever earns one. Found and fixed on the way: `Pid == Pid` on metal was hardcoded false — `emo_eq_deep`'s kind-18 arm now compares payloads, the language-level bug the demo's pid work surfaced. **The pins**: `run.sh`'s ordered chain grew `born` (the child re-enters the image's top level, finds the token the parent poked, prints through the gate) and `wait 7` (the parent's wait answered by the child's exit) under both timer transports, with the machine's self-exit; the reclamation is scheduler-internal, so it verifies under gdb against the golden's own image — at the exit's recording (a hardware watchpoint on the exit count), the child's slot (pid 8, slot 7) reads state 3 with the root still gate-waiting beside it (state 5, the answer in flight) and the services in their own states — a finished process holding no transaction. All twelve riscv64 QEMU goldens byte-identical; full `dune runtest` green.

- The weak-isolation window (EmoOS T4.5): **documented (2026-10-27) — `projects/emo-os/docs/isolation.md` (with the zh-CN mirror) is the standing record of what U-mode can and cannot reach pre-SV39, and the README's isolation-route bullet states the same in brief (EN + zh); no code moves, the goldens already pin the reach the document describes.** The account, in order. **The map is one flat table**: the firmware below 0x80200000 (the one region PMP locks), the kernel image with its 96 MiB heap and process world, the three fixed rings (the tick 0x87000000, the input 0x87001000, the handoff page 0x87002000), the user window [0x87100000, 0x88000000), and the MMIO beside the RAM. **What U-mode can reach**: every load and store, untranslated — kernel RAM, the rings, the handoff page, other processes' memory, the devices — with a wild jump into kernel text executing and staying in U-mode (privilege rides the mode, not the code); and the raw family (`peek`/`poke`, `ecall`, `device_wait`, and the T4.4 service words `user_spawn`/`gate_reply`/`pid_num`) is admitted by target, not profile — pre-SV39 the admission's own principle (a target admits the raw family iff the program owns the address space) is true of the hardware for a user image too, so refusing the spelling would hide the reach, not remove it; hello's greeting pokes the 16550A directly with no gate, and `run.sh` pins the line. **What U-mode cannot reach**: privileged instructions (every S-mode CSR, `sfence.vma`, `sret`) — today the loud failure ends the machine, T4.6 confines the kill to the offender; the firmware (a U-mode ecall is the kernel's door, never OpenSBI's, and the firmware's own region is the one range PMP keeps from lower privilege — PMP is M-mode-writable and OpenSBI defines no standard call to extend it); and the gate's refusals are answers, not faults. **The defense inventory meanwhile**: instruction privilege (the hardware's one line, holding absolutely), the gate's supervisor checks on every pointer argument, the copy semantics (messages are snapshots — aliasing through the mailbox is impossible, reaching by address is the window), and Step 5's per-process accounting; what does not defend — the MMU (absent), PMP for S-mode memory, and trap containment until T4.6. **Why accepted**: bounded in time (SV39 closes it, one milestone away on a fixed roadmap) and in kind (the long-term defenses are already the software contract; a program inside its own segments working through the gate is, in effect, isolated today).

- The kill (EmoOS T4.6, Step 4's close-out — M3): **implemented (2026-10-28) — a user fault kills its process alone, loudly, and the machine continues; a kernel fault stays the machine-wide failure.** The decisions, in order. **The split is the saved `sstatus.SPP`**: the shim's exception dispatch reads the privilege the trap came from off the frame — a fault from the kernel's own code (SPP = 1) is the kernel's bug and takes the loud failure that ends the machine, a fault from U-mode (SPP = 0) is the offender's alone. **The kill is the exit route's reclamation plus a report**: the shim calls the kernel's typed `fault_report(pid, scause, sepc)` on the offender's own stack — the gate's precedent, typed Emo inside the trap with zero allocation over the polled console — printing `user fault <pid> cause <scause> at <epc>`, marks the slot done (state 3), abandons the frame on the dead stack, and picks the next process; the root's death ends the machine as its exit does. The kill confines the *trap*, not the damage a wild store did before it — `isolation.md` says so. **The misbehaving program is a load the hardware refuses**: a misaligned `peek64` does not trap under QEMU (probed), so hello's second spawned pass loads `0x80000000` — the firmware's PMP-locked region, the one range PMP keeps from lower privilege, which also pins the isolation note's PMP claim — after claiming a `doomed` token on entry (two spawned passes share the image's top level; the claim is a clear) and printing its line through the gate; the parent never waits on it (a killed process never exited; its wait would block forever — the stated bound), and the good child's `born`/`wait 7` after the report is the survival proof. **Two latent faults the kill surfaced, both in the T4.4 runtime, both fixed**: ① `emo_gate_reply` opened a 16-byte frame and stored its second argument at `16(sp)`, eight bytes past the frame and into the replying service's own spill slot — a live local silently overwritten with the answer; the frame is 24 bytes. ② **The switch-in unmask window**: a trap frame's switch section carried the pre-trap SIE in its spare word, so `emo_sched_pick`'s unmask ran two instructions before the resume stub read the frame's rest section (`sstatus`, `sepc`, `ra`, the general registers) — an interrupt landing in that window laid its own 544-byte frame at `switch base − 544`, exactly over the rest section of the process being switched in, and the resume `sret`ted into garbage (the forensics: a trap frame found lying across the process table, `sstatus.FS` read back Off, the shim's own `fsd` faulting at every re-entry, sp at `0x6c90d000`). The window had always existed for every gate-park wake and every preemption, two instructions wide; the kill made it deterministic — `fault_report` prints for ~4 ms with SIE masked, so a timer is pending at the kill's pick and the next unmask takes it (1-in-3 failures). The rule now: **a trap frame switches in masked** — the spare word is 0 in the shim's frame and in `emo_user_spawn`'s synthetic frame — and its `sret` restores SIE from the saved SPIE, the one place that restore belongs; `emo_schedule`'s own frames keep recording their region's mask as before (T3.6), because their switch-in is a `ret` into kernel code, not a resume that still has a frame to read. The controls: 12-for-12 after the fix; a 10-round no-fault variant (two spawns, the doomed pass exiting normally) at 10-for-10, isolating the kill as the trigger; the T4.4 baseline's one failure in ten a different flake (below). **Also fixed: `run.sh`'s trace stream** — `-d int`'s dumps shared the serial's stream and split lines non-atomically (a byte could land inside a dump; no line-based cleanup could reassemble it), so the trace rides its own stream (`$log.trace`), the serial assertions read the pure serial, and the ecall count reads the trace; the old `.clean` view is gone (`.gitignore` follows). All twelve riscv64 QEMU goldens byte-identical through the two runtime fixes; full `dune runtest` green; `@fmt` green.

- Per-process arenas (EmoOS T5.1): **implemented (2026-10-28) — every allocation charges the current process's own arena, and a runaway process exhausts its quota loudly and alone.** The decisions, in order. **The arena lives in the slot**: the slot table grows two words — the arena cursor at 576 and the arena end at 584, the stride 576 → 592 — and `emo_alloc` bumps the current slot's pair instead of the one global cursor (the cursor variable is gone; `emo_heap_start`/`emo_heap_end` remain, now as the root arena's bounds, set by `emo_runtime_init`). **A child's arena is the root's top 64 KiB**: every spawn — green (`emo_spawn`) and user (`emo_user_spawn`) alike — carves from the root process's arena, its end closing above the carve and the child's pair covering exactly the range the root gave up, so the root can never allocate into a child's arena and a child's exhaustion is its own; the root's own cursor never moves, which is why the carve takes the top and not the cursor's edge (the first cut took the cursor's edge and dropped the root's end to its own cursor — the root could then never allocate again, and the golden died on the machine-wide message; the top-carve shape is the fix). The carve runs inside the spawn's masked region; no pool section, no link.ld change — the arenas live in the heap the image already has, and the remainder running out is the machine's loud end (`no arena left for a new process`), the process table's ceiling in memory. **Exhaustion is the profile's**: a kernel image owns its processes end to end — a spawned process whose arena ran dry dies alone, the loud report (`heap gone in process N`, a decimal printer the runtime grew) on the console, the slot ending done, `emo_sched_pick` continuing the machine (the fault route's reclamation, reached from the allocator; the region is masked, the dead frame stays on the dead stack, the arena unclaimed until T5.2 reclaims it) — while the root's exhaustion stays the machine-wide failure it always was. The user image's processes are the kernel's to track, so its `emo_alloc` says the gate's verb 5 instead: the shim's −104 route reports through the kernel's typed `heap_report(pid)` (`user heap <pid> gone`), ends the slot done, and picks — the root riding verb 5 ends the machine, as its exit does; `docs/syscalls.md` carries the verb (EN + zh). **The cross-image handoff**: the user image's runtime is a different instance whose allocator the kernel does not call — its stub parks the addresses of its own root slot's arena words in its cell page (0x87fff008/0x87fff010, beside `emo_program`), and the kernel's trap shim hands the arena across every U-mode boundary: a trap with SPP = 0 saves the cells' cursor into the leaver's slot, a resume with SPP = 0 writes the passer's cursor and end back, so the user image's `emo_alloc` (which reads its own slot table like any image) always charges the pass the kernel is actually running. **The boot pass is the exception that named a rule**: the first user pass is the kernel's own root still (the stub entered it after the kernel's program returned), and its progress must not land in the root slot — the spawn carve reads that slot's words as the kernel heap's — so pid 1's save and restore ride a dedicated boot pair in the kernel's `.bss` instead (the first cut poisoned the root slot with a user-heap cursor and the second user spawn refused loudly; the boot pair is the fix). **The kill's registers are the ABI's**: the report path keeps the slot pointer in `s0` and the pid on the stack across the print calls — the dying process's saved registers are the path's to spend (the fault route's precedent), because the callees own every t-register. Out of scope, stated: a user program mixing green threads (the user image's own scheduler) with gate spawn is not a shape this step makes claims about; reclamation on death, message-copy charging, and the frame pool are T5.2/T5.3. **The pins**: `examples/arena_gone` (a runaway green child burns its own 64 KiB and dies inside it — `heap gone in process 2` between the root's lines, `<pid 2>` after — riscv64 golden, beyond the interpreter like the rest of the raw-metal corpus); hello's third spawned pass (the greedy one) burns its carve and its allocator says verb 5 — `run.sh`'s ordered chain grew `greedy` and `user heap 9 gone` between the fault report and `born`/`wait 7`, under both timer transports, the good child's round trip riding after both deaths; the profile suite pins the arena words in the emitted runtime (the carve, both exhaustion tails, the user-only cells); the runtime suite is green with all twelve prior riscv64 QEMU goldens byte-identical, and the kernel smoke ran five consecutive rounds green on top of the suite's own two.

- Reclaim on death and the message copies (EmoOS T5.2): **implemented (2026-10-28) — a dead process's arena returns to the pool the moment nothing pins it, and a message crosses heaps twice: the send's snapshot charged to the sender, the receive's copy-out charged to the receiver.** The decisions, in order. **The pool is a freelist of 64 KiB blocks** (`emo_arena_pool`, the next link in a waiting block's first word): a spawn — green `emo_spawn` and user `emo_user_spawn` alike — pops a block first and carves from the root's arena top only when the pool is empty, so a dead process's arena is the next process's; the frame allocator (T5.3) generalizes the pool to stacks and page tables. **Reclaim is `emo_arena_reap(slot)` at every death**: the entry thunk's return (`emo_process_done`), the kernel profile's arena kill, and the shim's three death sites (the exit route, the fault kill, the heap kill) — it reclaims iff the slot is done, its arena still claimed (the new 600 word; the root's is never claimed), and nothing it sent is still queued (the new 592 count), else it returns and the death leaves the arena where it is. **The pin is the count of queued messages**: `emo_send` raises the sender's count at every enqueue and writes the sender's slot into a third mailbox ring (608..; the stride 592 → 864), and `emo_mb_remove` — now shifting all three rings — lowers that sender's count and reaps it, so a sender already done whose last queued message this was gets its arena back at the receive that cleared it, never earlier. A gate request posts a 0 into the sender ring (`emo_gate_post`): no sender, nothing pinned, nothing to unpin. **Copy-in is the send's snapshot** (T3.3's `emo_copy_deep`, in the sender's arena since T5.1 — the sender's quota pays for what it sends); **copy-out is the receive's `emo_copy_out`**, placed in the lowering between the pattern test and the bindings — the bindings destructure, so a copy at the remove would leave element words pointing into an arena the unpin may reclaim — cloning everything the snapshot shared: Int64/Float64 cells and Pids (the block clone with header 1), Strings (verbatim: bytes, not values), closures (the code word verbatim, the captures recursed), containers, and a Box by unbox/recurse/rebox (its payload rides the first word, which the block clone would copy verbatim). An Atomic alone stays the shared cell it is, and a shared Atomic never unpins: `emo_copy_out` sets a flag the remove reads, and the sender's arena stays pinned for good rather than dangling under the cell. **The sender ring, not the word, decides what is a value**: the first cut told gate requests from messages by height (`op << 32 | pid`, the services' own convention) and the kernel took an unexpected trap at the good child's exit — the exit is op 0, its request word the bare pid, and pid 10 reads as a Box pointer; `emo_copy_out_at(word, index)` reads the ring and passes a 0-sender word as-is. **Found on the way**: the clone loop ported from `emo_copy_deep` dropped its second bound store (the bound is `hdr + 1`), so the copy-out came one element short and the processes golden's tuple lost its second half — the deadlock after `4` named it. Stated bounds: a receiver dying with messages queued pins its senders' arenas for good (a leak, never a dangle); a receive nested inside a guard clobbers the shared-Atomic flag (pathological, unsupported); reclamation is O(1), a push. **The pins**: `examples/outlives` (a boxed message received after the sender's send, correct through the copy-out, the sender's arena home at its death — riscv64 golden, beyond the bare interpreter like every process example); all thirteen prior riscv64 QEMU goldens byte-identical; the kernel smoke's chain unchanged under both transports, five consecutive rounds green on top of the suite's own; and the forensics under gdb — on `arena_gone`, the kill's reap enters with `state 3, outstanding 0, claimed 1, pool nil` and leaves `pool = 0x86300000`, the dead child's block base (its arena end − 64 KiB); on `outlives`, the remove's reap enters with the sender still alive and returns, the pool staying nil; on the kernel, the four readers' deaths fill the pool LIFO (`0x862e0000` on top at the first user spawn), doomed pops reader 4's block, greedy pops reader 3's, and born pops `0x862e0000` again — doomed's own block, reclaimed at its kill and reused by the next spawn — the pool head landing on reader 2's block after. Full `dune runtest` green; `@fmt` green.

- The physical frame allocator (EmoOS T5.3): **implemented (2026-10-28) — the kernel owns its free RAM through one allocator written in Emo over raw memory, and every block a process holds — its arena, a user process's stack — is taken from it at the spawn and given back to it at the death.** The decisions, in order. **The allocator is the kernel's, not the runtime's**: `frames.emo` in the kernel package, the README's untyped-memory discipline (`Int64` addresses, `peek`/`poke`, bit masks), because physical memory is the kernel's to manage and a language runtime that owned the machine's frames would have the layering backwards; the runtime keeps T5.2's pool and T5.1's carve as the hookless default, so a plain program and a user image behave exactly as they did (every prior riscv64 golden byte-identical). **The unit is the 64 KiB block — sixteen frames of the architecture's 4 KiB page** — because that is the quantum both consumers take today (the arena, the stack), a freelist of blocks is O(1) both ways, and the page-table consumer (M5) splits a block into frames on the same page when it arrives; a 4 KiB freelist alone cannot answer a contiguous 64 KiB request, and a buddy or bitmap allocator earns nothing before single-frame consumers exist. **The region is the free RAM between the image's end and the first fixed page**: `__emo_bss_end` rounded up to a block (the boot stub passes the linker's symbol — the one address only it can name) to the tick ring at `0x87000000`; 139 blocks on today's image, more than the slot table's 64 spawns can hold at two blocks each even with nothing reclaimed, and an image grown past the region leaves an empty allocator whose first spawn is the loud end. The user window's top, where T4.4 bumped twelve unreclaimed stacks, goes back to the user image. **A give is strict**: a claimed bitmap on the allocator's page (`.emo_frames` at `0x87003000`, beside the head, the counts, the bounds, and the take/give tallies) must show the base claimed, aligned, and inside the region, or the machine ends with `frames: bad give` — a double give or a foreign base is a kernel bug, and a corrupted freelist would be the silent fallback this project refuses. **The runtime asks through two hooks**, the console hook's pattern (T3.5): `emo_block_take_hook` and `emo_block_give_hook`, two `.bss` words zero unless a kernel's stub parks the raw entries of `frames.take` and `frames.give`; `emo_spawn` and `emo_user_spawn` take the arena block through the first when it is parked (spilling the slot and its index across the call — a typed Emo callee owns every t- and a-register), and `emo_arena_reap` — a leaf no longer — gives through the second under T5.2's pin rule, the pool push only when no hook is there. **The slot remembers the stack block**: a new word at 864 (the stride 872), the base of the block whose top the kernel handed `emo_user_spawn`, 0 for the root and for a green process; the reaper gives it back at every death unconditionally and first — nothing pins a stack — then the arena under its pin. Green processes' stacks stay their slots' own in `emo_pstacks`: a slot is never reused, so neither is its stack, and routing them would reclaim nothing. **The process service takes before it spawns**, and refuses (reason 5) when the allocator holds fewer than the two blocks a pass costs — a spawn the allocator cannot back is the caller's refusal, never a machine the runtime's take ends. **Bounded caches for long-lived services** are the discipline this makes possible: a service's working set is the blocks it holds and can give back, never a bump heap that only grows — the kernel's two services keep one arena each for the whole run and allocate nothing per request (typed bodies over raw words), and their bookkeeping stays fixed cells. **Nothing in the module allocates**: every def is a native body over raw words, because the hooks run inside the scheduler's masked regions and the account inside the trap; verified on the image — no `emo_alloc` reachable from the module, the const thunks included. **The machine's last line is the account**: `frames <taken> <given>`, printed by the shim's exit route and the stub's own shutdown, so every end the kernel owns ends with its memory's books. Stated bounds: the allocator's operations run unmasked in a service body on the dormant-quota guarantee (no preemption lands between a service's test and its take; the ISR never allocates), and the masked regions hold the hook calls — the first settable quota revisits this; a double give is caught, a use after give is not. **The pins**: `run.sh`'s ordered chain ends with `frames 12 10` under both timer transports — six green spawns' arenas plus a stack and an arena for each of three user passes taken, the four readers' arenas and both blocks of each user pass given back, the two services keeping theirs; the forensics under gdb (breakpoints on `take` and `give` through a full boot) show the six takes walking up from the region's base, the readers' deaths giving back, doomed's and greedy's spawns taking the four returned blocks (the doomed pass parks at its gate line, so the parent's second spawn lands before the fault), doomed's kill giving its two back, and **born's two takes being exactly doomed's two blocks** — a killed pass's memory serving the next spawn — then the tallies landing on 12 and 10; the profile suite pins the hooks, the stride, the slot's stack word, and the hookless pool path in both profiles' emitted runtime. Full `dune runtest` green with the kernel smoke; `@fmt` green.

- The accounting goldens (EmoOS T5.4): **implemented (2026-10-29) — the quota story is pinned end to end: a runaway process exhausts its own 64 KiB and dies alone while the processes around it keep spending their own quotas across its death. Step 5 complete — M4.** The golden the set was missing is `examples/survivors`: three children spawn against three arenas — two spenders with the runaway between them — and the ordering is the scheduler's own determinism (every quota 0, no preemption, FIFO): the root parks at its receive, the children run in spawn order, the runaway burns its carve and dies between the spenders' turns, so its report (`heap gone in process 3`) reaches the serial before the root's first receive answers — and of the four boxed messages the root collects, only the first (101) was allocated before the death; 301, 102, and 302 are boxes a survivor allocated after the runaway was already gone, each send's snapshot charged to its sender's arena, each copy-out landing in the root's heap, the root summing all four (`total 806`) and ending the machine itself. The set this completes: `arena_gone` (a lone runaway, the root surviving), the kernel chain's greedy pass (the user image's exhaustion through the gate's verb 5), `outlives` (a message correct through a dead sender), and the `frames 12 10` account — survivors is the middle piece, the other quotas unaffected and still spending across a death. **Found and fixed, the golden's first boot caught it — a compiler bug as old as the native bodies (T2.2)**: mixed-type arithmetic inside a native body (`total = total + b`, `b` a receive pattern binding) — `emit_binary`'s dynamic-operand branch answers the tagged word the `emo_*_dyn` helper returns, but the representation rule promises an expression the checker types Int64 produces the raw payload in `a0`, so the assignment stored the result cell's address as the value and a two-message probe printed `2150695040` where `402` was true. The kernel never hit it — its receive branches keep arithmetic in typed helpers and fixed cells, never a bare mixed-type sum stored to a numeric local. The fix is the Call arm's existing rule applied to the dynamic arithmetic: after the `emo_add/sub/mul_dyn` call, `unbox_if_numeric` delivers the raw payload when the expression is numeric (comparisons answer Bool, whose tagged word is already the convention; dynamic bodies are untouched — `boxed` holds). Pins: the example registered in the interpreter-skip list and the riscv64 golden list; three consecutive boots byte-identical; full `dune runtest` green with all fifteen riscv64 QEMU goldens byte-identical through the fix; the kernel smoke unchanged and green under both timer transports.

- Sv39 and the kernel's high-half layout (EmoOS T6.1): **implemented (2026-10-29) — the kernel runs under Sv39 in the upper half of the address space, on a page table it builds for itself in Emo before its first line; the structures are raw memory, the two privileged instructions are shims, and the window's reach is unchanged until the per-process tables.** The decisions, in order. **The kernel lives in the high half, by one offset**: every kernel address is the physical one plus `koff` = `0xffffffc000000000`, the base of Sv39's upper half — as an `Int64` −2³⁸, so a kernel address is negative and a user address positive, the sign a test no table lookup beats — and the whole kernel world moves there: the image (`link.ld` links at the high alias of `0x80200000` with every section's LMA physical, the ELF entry the physical alias since QEMU enters at the load base), the fixed pages and rings, the devices, and the allocator's region, whose bounds, links, and answers (`frames.emo`) are kernel addresses throughout. Chosen over the identity map the window ran on because the user then owns the whole lower half (one fixed load address for every ELF, T6.2), kernel mappings can be shared into any address space without colliding, and the layout is the one T6.2's "kernel pages without U" and T6.3's trampoline both assume. **The structures are Emo over raw memory** (`sv39.emo`, the README's untyped-memory discipline — `Int64` addresses, `peek`/`poke`, bit masks, every def a native body): an entry is `PPN << 10 | flags`, the walk takes nine bits per level from the root, `map` writes a leaf at any level with A and D set (the kernel keeps no use counts; an implementation trapping on a clear bit must not get the chance), `map_range` chooses megapages wherever both addresses sit on a 2 MiB boundary with 2 MiB to go and 4 KiB pages elsewhere, `lookup` is the software walk, and the rules are strict — a leaf met on the way down, an address mapped twice, a leaf or range off its page boundary are each the loud end, never a silent overwrite. **The kernel's table**: text RX, rodata R, data onward RW to the RAM's top (the linker script aligns and exports the two boundaries; the stub passes them), the UART's page and the PLIC's 4 MiB RW, and the firmware in no kernel mapping (the SBI calls the kernel still makes are M-mode ecalls, untranslated); the builder walks its own table for the addresses the next instructions depend on and dies with a line rather than fault at the satp write. Five frames, one block — the root, the RAM's level-1, the image head's level-0, the MMIO's level-1, the UART page's level-0 — so `frames.emo` grew `frame_take`, the 4 KiB unit split sixteen off a block onto its own freelist on the allocator's page (a block once split stays split: the tables' working set is small and long-lived). **The shims are `sv39.S`**: `emo_os_satp_switch` (a root's physical address; fence, the write, fence; the value written answered) and `emo_os_sfence` — the gate's division of labor, Emo building and reading tables, the shim writing the CSR no Emo spelling reaches; the stub is the only caller now, the context switch and the trampoline next. **The boot needs a boot table**: the Emo that builds the real table is itself linked high and reads the allocator's page by a kernel address, so not one instruction of it could run untranslated; the stub, at the physical alias (`la` is PC-relative), clears `.bss`, writes three gigapage entries into a zeroed page — RAM at identity so its own next fetches land, RAM and the first gigabyte of MMIO at the high alias — turns satp on, and jumps to the high alias of the next instruction; everything after is the high half, and once `sv39.boot` answers, the shim switches, the stub keeps the satp read-back on a new fixed page (`.emo_sv39` at `0x87004000`, the root beside it), and sets SUM. **The window is now a mapping, with the reach it had**: two gigapage entries with U — RAM at identity RWX (the user image executes there), the first gigabyte of MMIO RW (the first user program's greeting pokes the UART) — so `isolation.md`'s account stands word for word (updated to say the window is these two entries), the kill golden's cause 5 stands (the firmware is in the window's gigapage, PMP still refuses), and the task closes nothing: T6.2's per-process tables do. Chosen over narrowing the window here because a partial narrowing would write an intermediate state into the isolation record that T6.2 rewrites, and because a byte-identical chain under a relinked kernel is the strongest proof a relocation can have. **Two aliases, two explicit translation points**: the kernel reaches user memory by the user's addresses with SUM (the gate's buffers, the loader's copies, the trap frames the shim lays on a user stack, the synthetic frame a spawn builds — `user.emo`'s window constants stay physical on purpose); the arena handoff at the user's cells (`trap.S`) adds `koff` on the trap out of U-mode and subtracts it on the way in, the slot's words kernel addresses and the cells the user's, the boot pass's pair round-tripping the user's values as they are; and `frames.give` translates a physical base to the kernel's before its strict checks — the runtime names a dead pass's stack by the address the pass ran on (`user_spawn` receives the identity-alias top; the process service passes `sv39.pa_of(stack) + block`), and the one place the two aliases meet is written down. T6.2 ends the alias and both points. **Found on the first spawn — SUM is the kernel's, not the frame's**: the runtime's synthetic frame carries a bare `sstatus` (`0x6020`, SPIE and FS), so the first resume of a spawned pass cleared SUM, and the pass's next trap faulted at the shim's first frame store onto the user stack — a store page fault every 544 bytes down the stack, the storm the trace showed after `echo 88`; the resume stub now ORs SUM into every restored `sstatus`, so no frame's word decides what the kernel may reach. **The pins**: `run.sh`'s chain opens with `sv39 8 0x8…` — `sv39_report` reads the live satp back, requires mode 8 and the root the builder recorded on the Sv39 page, prints the line or ends the machine (`sv39: the live table is not the kernel's`) — and closes with `frames 13 10`, the page tables' block beside the twelve, the rest of the chain byte-identical to the pre-T6.1 chain under both timer transports, three consecutive rounds identical; the kernel could not run at all untranslated, so the chain is itself the proof satp is on, and the line is the proof the table running is the Emo-built one; QEMU's monitor after the boot reads back exactly the eight mappings designed (`0x0`→`0x0` 1 GiB `rw-u`, `0x80000000` identity 1 GiB `rwxu`, the PLIC's 4 MiB and the UART's page `rw-`, text 32 KiB `r-x`, rodata 112 KiB `r--`, the image's RW head in 4 KiB pages, then `0x80400000`–`0x88000000` in megapages `rw-`) and `satp 0x800000000008676f`, `stvec` high, SUM set; full `dune runtest` green (the `emo_os` suite's scratch tree carries the two new files), `@fmt` green, no compiler change. The layout's standing record is `projects/emo-os/docs/sv39.md` (with the zh-CN mirror); the README (EN + zh) gains the high-half bullet and the status sentence.

- Per-process address spaces and the pick's switch (EmoOS T6.2): **implemented (2026-10-29) — every user process, the boot pass included, runs paged on its own Sv39 table built in Emo at the spawn, and the context switch is the scheduler's pick resolving the incoming slot's space word through the `satp` shim; the weak-isolation window is closed.** The decisions, in order. **The tables are `space.emo`, the runtime asks through three hooks**: the block hooks' pattern again (T3.5/T5.3) — `emo_space_build_hook` (the build, called at the end of `emo_user_spawn` with the dressed slot: pid, arena, stack block, synthetic frame, saved sp all in it), `emo_space_hook` (the resolve, called by the pick with the incoming slot's word), and `emo_space_write_hook` (parked at `emo_os_satp_switch`); all three `.bss` words zero in every image whose stub leaves them — plain programs and the user image never touch `satp` — and the whole switch is two loads in images without per-process spaces. **What a pass's table maps**: the kernel's whole half without U (`sv39.map_kernel`, factored from the boot builder, which now records the linker's permission boundaries on the Sv39 page so every table draws the same picture), the image's segments with U — permissions translated from the loader's parked ELF table, the boundary page where text and data share a 4 KiB page taking the union (`map_page` in space.emo; two segments claiming one page at different physical addresses is the loud end), — the user cells page, the pass's own stack and arena blocks at the identity of their physical addresses, and the root's physical address into the slot's new space word at 872 (stride 880; 0 = the kernel's own table). U-mode's world is exactly those pages; everything else is an unmapped fault or mapped without U, and the passes of one image still share its pages — the spawn semantics since T4.4 (a spawned pass re-enters the top level and finds the tokens the parent poked) survive intact. **The kernel's own table carries no U anywhere now, and gains the supervisor's low window**: the load address to the RAM's top at identity, RW, no U, megapages — the loader's copies, the handoff cells, the process service's read of the parked program cell, and the gate's answers into parked trap frames are all low addresses, and a kernel process runs on this table; the T6.1 U gigapages are gone. The firmware's region stays in no mapping (the window begins at the load address). **A user table carries no low window**: the supervisor's window is megapages and the user's pages are 4 KiB ones in the same half of the same tree — a table carrying both died of "a leaf blocks the walk" on the first boot — and a table the kernel runs on inside a trap needs nothing the pass's own mappings lack (everything the shim touches is either high or the caller's own mapped pages). **The switch is the pick's, and nowhere else**: `emo_sched_pick` resolves the incoming word (0 → the kernel's root; a cache on the Sv39 page's third word answers −1 when `satp` already names it, so the kernel's processes switch among themselves fencelessly), writes on a root, all before a register of the incoming process is loaded and inside the masked region; the trampoline (T6.3) refines the crossing without moving any of this. **The boot pass's table is built at stage time** — the loader builds it after parking the segment table and before parking the entry (a failed build leaves the entry zero, the machine never enters U-mode), parks its physical root in the handoff page's space cell (`0x870021c8`), and the enter shim takes it into slot 0's space word (`emo_space_set`, a new runtime entry), adopts it into the switch's cache (`space.adopt`), and switches before the `sret` — the word is in the slot before the first trap can preempt the pass. **The gate's write copies at the gate**: the check and the copy-in are one trap (the send snapshot's discipline) — bytes land in a staging page on the handoff page (2048-byte capacity, over-long is reason 4 before anything moves), and the console service prints from the kernel's copy on its own table, which maps none of the caller's memory; the direct read-through-the-window the service did is retired with the window, and the read-after-check race it carried with it. **The greeting rides the gate**: a pass's table maps no UART, so hello's direct 16550A pokes became `syscall.write` rides — the same bytes on the wire, the weak window's last golden retired on purpose. **Found and fixed on the way, the first boot caught it: the synthetic frame's zero loop was off by one** — `emo_user_spawn` cleared 552 bytes for a 544-byte frame (`ble` where the bounds say `blt`), writing eight zero bytes past the stack top, and the word past a pass's stack top is the next 64 KiB block's freelist link; since T4.4 the extra word always landed in a claimed block's first cell and stayed invisible, but the boot pass's table took one more block and moved the adjacency onto the free list — the allocator's chain lost its head to eight zeros (`no arena left` at the greedy spawn; the ledger and watchpoints under gdb named the block, the write, and the `ble`). Found and fixed on the way: the pick's hook calls clobbered a1 (caller-saved, and the resolve is ordinary typed Emo whose own calls spend it freely) — the slot pointer rides s1, a register the outgoing context has already surrendered to its frame. **The kill golden's cause moved with the tables**: the doomed pass's load at `0x80000000` is now the load page fault, cause 13 — its own table maps nothing there, where the window's RAM gigapage made the same access PMP's access fault, cause 5 — and the fault route needed no change. **The pins**: `run.sh`'s chain under both timer transports, three consecutive rounds identical — unchanged except the kill line (`cause 13`) and the last line (`frames 17 10`, the thirteen plus the four blocks the tables' frames split, kept for the whole run like the services' arenas); QEMU's monitor reads the kernel's table back as seven mappings, not one carrying U, the supervisor's window identity `rw-` beginning at the load address; `dune runtest` green with the stride pin at 880, the space-hook pins in both profiles (the three words, the pick's resolve, the build call, the slot table's grown size), and the `emo_os` suite's scratch tree carrying `space.emo`; `@fmt` green. The standing records: `projects/emo-os/docs/sv39.md` grew the per-process section (zh-CN mirror updated), `docs/isolation.md` is rewritten as the closed window's record (zh-CN mirror), `docs/syscalls.md` states the staging bound and the copy-in (zh-CN mirror), and the READMEs (EN + zh) state the per-process tables.
- The trap-entry trampoline (EmoOS T6.3): **implemented (2026-10-30) — a trap out of U-mode no longer obeys anything the pass chose: the entry frames at a kernel-chosen anchor, swaps `satp` through the switch's own resolve/write pair, and runs the kernel's dispatch on the kernel's own table and stack; the crossing has a page of its own.** The decisions, in order. **The trampoline is a page, and it is the one mapping the crossing trusts**: `link.ld` gives the `.emo_tramp` section a page-aligned page at the end of the text, alone on it, and `map_kernel`'s text RX carries it into every table without U — mapped for the supervisor's fetch, unreachable from U-mode, the same virtual address in every space, which is what lets one `stvec` serve both worlds. **The path is chosen by a swap, not by a branch's register**: `sscratch` holds the running pass's trap anchor while user code runs (the identity address of its fixed trap frame, armed by every return into U-mode) and the sentinel 0 while kernel code runs, so `csrrw sp, sscratch, sp` costs no register — a user trap finds the anchor in sp and saves all 32 registers before one is spent; a kernel trap finds 0, undoes the swap with the second `csrrw`, and the shim runs byte-for-byte as it always has, framing on the interrupted kernel stack. The discipline is closed: the sentinel is set at trap init, re-armed by the trampoline's user path after the sp is recovered, by the shim's path, and by the resume's kernel-path return; the anchor is armed at the boot pass's entry and at every user return. **The anchor is memory no execution uses for live data** — a spawned pass anchors at the bottom of its own stack block (the 544-byte frame at block+552, the interrupted-sp slot in the word below; beneath any call chain the block's top can grow, since a descent that deep is the overflow the block's edge used to swallow), and the boot pass anchors in its own trap page (`0x87ffe000`, the fixed page under the cells page, mapped rw without U into every table by `space.base` — the image's stack belongs to the stub and the heap is live data, so the page is the scratch that is the boot pass's alone). The frame region is scratch while the pass runs and the frame while it is stopped — a pass cannot run and be stopped at once, so nothing user-side can reach a parked frame, and the next trap rewrites every word of it before any is read. A wild sp is saved and handed back, never obeyed: the kernel stores nothing through an address the pass chose. **The transition's middle swaps `satp` through the pick's own pair** — the trampoline resolves word 0 and calls the write hook, so the Sv39 page's cache stays true about what `satp` holds (a raw `csrw` here would make the pick's cache lie and let a kernel process run on a user's table); after the swap the dispatch runs on the kernel's table **and the kernel's trap stack** (8 KiB of `.bss`), reading the frame through `emo_os_trap_frame`, the base both prologues park — never through sp, whose value now differs by path, and never through the pass's memory. **The sret home is the resume's**: its user path brings the pass's table back through the same resolve/write pair — a resume the answer and home paths reach directly rides no pick, and the trampoline left the kernel's table live, so without this the `sret` landed in U-mode on the kernel's table, an instruction page fault at the pass's own text — re-arms the anchor, restores every register while sp is still the frame's top, and moves sp **last**, by its own self-relative load from the slot below the frame; the interrupted sp the pass chose is returned verbatim. The synthetic frame moved with the anchor: `emo_user_spawn` builds it at the block's bottom and parks the initial sp in the slot, so the pass enters U-mode at the top of its stack and its first trap lands on its own frame. **Found on the way, the chain caught all three design bites**: the first sketch framed at the stack's top and derived the boot pass's anchor from the segment table's end — the frame landed on the user image's slot table (live state; `emo_alloc` died with a store fault in the pass) and, worse, on any pass's own live call frames once its depth passed the frame's height, so the anchor moved to memory no execution reaches; the dispatch first ran at sp = the frame base and its own calls wrote return addresses into the interrupted-sp slot the resume reads (and parking the frame base in `sscratch` during the handler would have leaked a stale anchor across the pick into kernel mode, where the next kernel trap would have taken the user path), so the base moved to the word and the handler to the kernel's stack; and the resume's first tail restored `t0`/`t1` after moving sp to the user's value — the loads read the user's stack, not the frame, and the stub's sp region sat in the `.bss` its clear loop had just zeroed, so `t0` came back 0 and the pass's first store faulted — the frame reads happen while sp is still the frame's top, and the sp moves last. **The pins**: `run.sh`'s chain byte-identical to T6.2's under both timer transports, three consecutive rounds — the crossing changed, not the behavior; `frames 17 10` unchanged (the trampoline costs no allocation); QEMU's monitor reads `stvec` at the trampoline page's address, page-aligned, `sscratch` 0 while a kernel process runs, and the seven mappings with the text one page longer; watchpoints at the anchor catch the save landing there with `sscratch` holding the interrupted user sp; `dune runtest` green — 333, with the new both-profiles pin (the anchor arithmetic `addi t4, t6, 552`, the initial sp parked `sd t5, -8(t4)`); `@fmt` green. The standing records: `projects/emo-os/docs/sv39.md` grew the trampoline section (zh-CN mirror updated), `docs/isolation.md` notes the trap entry among the defenses (zh-CN mirror), and the READMEs (EN + zh) state the trampoline.

- Page faults (EmoOS T6.4): **implemented (2026-10-30) — a page fault is a kill, not a question, and the kill names its address: `stval` rides the trap frame to the report, a store where the table maps nothing kills its pass as loudly as a load does, and nothing anywhere fills the page.** The decisions, in order. **The kill machinery is the fault route's, unchanged**: the three page-fault causes (instruction fetch 12, load 13, store 15) arrive like any exception the gate does not own, T4.6's SPP split sends a U-mode offender to the report-and-die path (slot done, frame abandoned, blocks home, machine continuing) and an S-mode miss to the machine-wide loud failure — a supervisor page fault is a kernel bug, never a process's, because the kernel runs on tables it built itself. What T6.4 adds is the name: **both prologues save `stval`** — the shim's and the trampoline's, beside `scause` and `sepc` in the rest section's one free word (offset 328, past `fa7`, the only slot the 336-byte section still had) — and the route passes it as `fault_report`'s fourth word: `user fault <pid> cause <c> at 0x<epc> addr 0x<stval>`. For a page fault it is the address the walk never reached; for the other exceptions it is whatever the hardware recorded (an illegal instruction's bits, or zero) — printed as it comes, no interpretation. The address is asserted in the chain where the epc is not: the epc moves per build, the address is the program's own constant. **The store-fault pass (`smash`)**: hello's fourth spawned pass, between the runaway and the good child (it runs while the parent parks at the good child's spawn — the doomed pass's discipline), says its line through the gate, then one `poke64` at `0x40000000`, an address its own table maps nowhere — the store page fault, cause 15, `addr 0x40000000` in the report; the parent does not wait on it, as it does not on the doomed pass (a killed process never exited — the stated bound). Chosen over extending the doomed pass's own branch so each fault stays its own pass's one misdeed, the reports one per line, and the survival proof (the good child's round trip) answers after all three deaths. **Demand paging is out of scope by decision and by construction**: nothing inspects the address to fill a mapping — no demand paging, no copy-on-write, no swap; there is no consumer of a filled page yet, and a handler that could invent mappings would be the one component the tables' strictness (map twice, a leaf on the walk, off-boundary — each the loud end) could not reach. The ledger moved with the fourth pass: `frames 19 12` — its stack and arena taken and given back at its kill, and its table's frames drawn from the 4 KiB leftovers of the earlier tables' splits (a block once split stays split, T6.1), no block of its own. The pins: `run.sh`'s chain under both timer transports, three consecutive rounds — grown by `smash` and its report between the runaway's and the good child's lines, both fault reports naming their addresses (`addr 0x80000000`, `addr 0x40000000`, both asserted as ordered lines); `dune runtest` green with the smoke; `@fmt` green. The standing records: `projects/emo-os/docs/sv39.md` grew the page-faults section (zh-CN mirror updated), and the READMEs (EN + zh) state the kill-named-by-address.

- Isolation goldens (EmoOS T6.5): **implemented (2026-10-30) — the SV39 closure is pinned from both ends: the store at a kernel address dies of the permission the walk refuses (the mapping is there, without U), and after a whole boot's traffic the allocator still answers one whole physically contiguous 64 KiB block. Step 6 complete — M5.** The decisions, in order. **The kernel-address golden is a fifth killed pass** (`reach`, the doomed/smash discipline: its line through the gate, one store, no wait — a killed process never exited) whose store lands at the kernel's own image by its high-half address (`0x80200000 - 0x4000000000`): chosen over the trampoline page or a random kernel address because the image is the canonical "kernel's memory" — and the shape is deliberately the OTHER failure from the smash pass's: the mapping exists in the pass's table (the kernel's half, no U), the walk reaches the leaf, and it is the permission that refuses — the same cause 15 as an unmapped store, the reason different, the report's named address the tell. **The hex printer is unsigned-faithful now**: a kernel address is negative as an `Int64`, and `fault_hex`'s divide-by-16 recursion would have printed sign-mangled digits or run away at −1 — the printer walks from the top instead (`(n >> shift) & 15` for shift 60 down to 0, leading zeros skipped, the shift==0 digit always printed), the arithmetic shift keeping every nibble true and the sign bits being the top digits; every prior positive output is byte-identical (the epc and addr assertions, the sv39 root, all unchanged). **The DMA golden is the machine's last act**: the exit route calls the kernel's typed `dma_report` before the frames account — `take()` must answer a block (the loud end otherwise), the base must be block-aligned, a distinct pattern word lands in each of the block's sixteen 4 KiB frames and all sixteen read back (the range is one piece of writable RAM, not sixteen addresses that happen to answer — an alias inside the range would fail the readback), and the block goes home; the line `dma 0x…0000 ok` rides the chain before `frames`, and the check's own take-and-give is on the ledger the account prints right after — the proof and its cost on the same wire. **The discipline this pins for M6**: a device's DMA buffer comes from `take` — the 64 KiB block, physically contiguous by the allocator's unit and never fragmented by the 4 KiB splits (a block once split stays split, T6.1) — never from sixteen `frame_take`s; when the virtio-blk service arrives it takes blocks, and the contract it will rely on is already on the record. The check runs in the exit route's trap, the fault report's discipline: typed Emo, raw words, zero allocation, `take`/`give` the allocator's own account (the fault route's reaper precedent). The ledger moved with the fifth pass and the check: `frames 23 15` — the reach pass's stack and arena (given back at its kill), its table splitting the fifth table block (the splits' leftovers that carried the smash pass's table ran out), and the check's own pair. The pins: `run.sh`'s chain under both timer transports, three consecutive rounds — `reach` and its report between the smash pass's and the good child's lines, the kernel address asserted in full (`addr 0xffffffc080200000`), `dma 0xffffffc08` and `0000 ok` asserted in order, the good child's round trip after the four deaths; `docs/isolation.md`'s cannot-reach bullet names the three pinned kills (zh-CN mirror), `docs/sv39.md`'s outstanding section closes Step 6 (zh-CN mirror), the READMEs state the closure and the buffer and the milestone table turns M5 complete (EN + zh). `dune runtest` green with the smoke; `@fmt` green.

- The virtio-blk driver as a process (EmoOS T7.1): **implemented (2026-10-30) — a kernel Emo process owns the block device end to end: it finds it on the virtio-mmio buses, negotiates the modern layout, builds its split virtqueue on one allocator block, and answers sector writes and reads from its mailbox; the golden is one sector out through the queue and back (`blk 2048 ready`, `blk rw ok`), and the machine's ledger carries the driver's arena and queue block (`frames 25 15`).** The decisions, in order. **The driver is the services' shape**: a green process spawned third, announcing its pid, its whole state body locals across the receives; the request word packs `op << 32 | sector`, a write's data word follows as its own message, the reply is a message back — no fixed cells, no gate verbs (the FS service's surface is T7.2's to design). **The slot scan, not a fixed address**: QEMU's `-device virtio-blk-device` lands on the virtio-mmio buses by enumeration (bus 7 today — and plain `-drive if=virtio` lands on PCIe instead, the monitor caught it), so the driver scans the eight transports for DeviceID 2 — the FDT walk's shape, immune to the enumeration. **The modern layout, negotiated properly**: reset → ACK → DRIVER → `VIRTIO_F_VERSION_1` alone in the high feature word (the selector-then-value pairs cannot ride one 64-bit poke — the value would land before its selector — so the two pokes order the banks) → FEATURES_OK verified → the queue's three addresses registered (Desc/Avail/Used Low-then-High, each pair one `poke64`: both halves are plain address registers, ascending) → QueueReady → DRIVER_OK; run.sh pins `-global virtio-mmio.force-legacy=false` so the transports report version 2, and a transitional device would meet the driver's loud end. **The queue is one 64 KiB block** — the T6.5 discipline made real: desc table, avail ring, used ring, the request's header, data buffer, and status byte all inside the one contiguous range, every device-visible address physical, the block a service's working set held for its life. One request in flight at a time (descriptors 0..2 reused), the doorbell after `fence()`. **The completion wait is polled** — the console's pre-T3.7 discipline, bounded with the loud end: the interrupt-driven wait needs a park that checks a condition cell inside the scheduler's masked region (the check-then-park race the ISR's wake cannot close), a runtime piece whose demand is the FS service's concurrency (T7.2); the virtio IRQ stays unwired and the external handler still refuses every IRQ it does not know. **Three finds, two compiler bugs fixed**: the kernel's table now maps the eight virtio transport pages (`map_kernel`, every table's picture, no U — the driver's first peek faulted without them); **`peek32`/`poke32` joined the raw family** — byte-composed 32-bit MMIO writes are illegal on modern virtio (every byte offset is its own register access; the unmapped neighbors faulted the device into a reset — status read 0, the trace named the offsets), and a 64-bit poke would touch the neighbor register, so ordinary code could not express a four-byte device write and the width earned its line: `Int64 → Int64` zero-extended and `Int64 → Int64 → Void` with the value runtime-guarded to 0–4294967295 (`Byte.from_int64`'s loud-never-silent rule; the `Int32` arithmetic type stays deferred — the line is the device lane, not the arithmetic), checker and IR and interpreter refusal and both riscv64 paths (inline `lw`/`sw` native, boxed helpers dynamic), the refusal suite extended; and **`Assign_var` no longer flips a local's slot convention at an assignment** — a numeric local declared raw, reassigned a tagged word inside a conditional, read after the conditional: the old emitter removed the name from the raw set at the assignment's emission, so the skipped path read the declaration's raw word with tagged semantics and dereferenced address 8 (tval named it; the read request skipping the write's data receive was the trap) — the convention is fixed at the declaration (or the param list) and never moves, every store converting the produced word into the slot's convention (raw↔tagged by the value's ety, the representation rule's both directions). Pins: `run.sh`'s chain under both timer transports, three consecutive rounds — the block lines in place, the pass pids shifted one (a third service), `frames 25 15`; the disk a 1 MiB raw image run.sh creates when absent, gitignored; `docs/raw-memory.md` states the 32-bit line (zh-CN mirror); the READMEs state the block device (EN + zh). Full `dune runtest` green with the smoke; `@fmt` green.
- The filesystem service (EmoOS T7.2): **implemented (2026-10-30) — the service that owns the disk's layout, on T7.1's driver, designed first (`projects/emo-os/docs/fs.md`, zh-CN mirror alongside) around the split the kickoff asked for: the disk is the authoritative state and the service's memory is rebuilt from it at every start; a crash of the service costs the clients it left parked a refusal and nothing else, and the kernel brings the service back by itself.** The decisions, in order. **The split**: authoritative is sector 0's superblock (the mount table in it), the name table, and the data sectors — nothing else; rebuildable is everything in the service's memory — the copies, the free map, the request bookkeeping — derived from the disk at the start and trusted from nothing else. **Write-through, never write-back**: every change reaches the disk inside the request that makes it, so an answered write is on the disk and there is no flush verb because nothing is ever pending. **The free map is rebuildable-only** — a function of the name table (every named extent used, everything else in the data region free), rebuilt at every mount and never written to the disk, where a second copy would be a second authority. **The mount table is the superblock's record of the volumes served** — one entry today (`root`: its name table at sectors 1–8, its data region from 9 to the end), read back by every instance and drawn from no constant of the service's but the format's; durable because it is on the disk and nowhere else, so an instance after a crash or a power cycle knows what it serves unasked; no device field until a second disk earns it. **The format is the first mount's own act** on a disk without the magic (`EmoFS-01`), never a tool, never on a disk that carries the magic. **The commit point is one sector write**: a write allocates in the memory map, writes the bytes to fresh sectors (the last one's tail zero), writes the entry's sector, then frees the old extent in memory — a crash before the entry write leaves the old file and unreferenced sectors the rebuild frees, a crash after leaves the new file; the disk never names bytes it does not hold. The one assumption underneath is a 512-byte sector write's atomicity, stated as such. **A transaction is one request** — whole-file write, read, list, remove; no handles, so a restart invalidates nothing a client holds. A flat namespace, a file one contiguous extent, 64 entries, 24-byte names, 2048 bytes per write: what the shell needs and nothing it does not. **What a death is**: a fault in a kernel service is the kernel's fault and the machine's end (T4.6's split on SPP) — the deaths a service dies alone are the arena kill (no collector; a long-lived service reaches it, and the restart turns the limit into a maintenance event) and its own return (the stop a kernel client asks for, op 0 of the kernel-side six-tuple protocol), and both meet one mechanism. **The death hook** is a runtime word (`emo_death_hook`, the console/block/space hooks' pattern — zero in every image whose stub leaves it) called with the dead pid at the two places a green process ends alone, the entry thunk's return and the kernel profile's arena kill, after the slot is marked done and before the reclamation; the kernel's stub parks a trap-shim routine there that clears the filesystem's pid cell when the dead pid is the service's (inside the death's own masked region, so nothing is ever posted to a dead mailbox) and posts `256 << 32 | pid` to the supervisor's mailbox the way the gate posts a request. **The supervisor** is a kernel process the root spawns once: it takes the service's cache block from the allocator once and hands the same block to every instance (the block outlives them; each overwrites it from the disk), spawns the service, parks the announced pid in the cell the gate routes by, forwards the Pid to the root, and on a notice naming the current instance refuses every client the dead one left parked — a busy request slot is always a gate caller parked in state 5, so `gate_reply(-5)` is always safe there — clears the slots and the stash, spawns the next, and parks its pid. **The gate's side**: verbs 6–9 continue the table (reason 6, `no such file`, joins the ABI), every pointer argument checked and copied at the gate into one of four request slots on the filesystem's own pages (`.emo_fs`, three pages from `0x87005000`, the layout in the note), the slot's index riding the route above the op (`-(1000 + op + 16 × slot)`, the request word `slot << 36 | op << 32 | pid`;
